# ISO 15118-2:2014 — normative rule book for charger fault detection

Extracted from the standard by seven parallel readers, each assigned a
different clause group, then re-read by an adversarial verifier whose job
was to find the number in the text itself before confirming it and to
report anything the extractor missed.

Statements are paraphrased. Only requirement ids, constant names, enum
literals, table numbers and numeric values are reproduced verbatim —
the standard itself is copyrighted and is not redistributed here.

- rules extracted: **773**, surviving verification: **773**
- verification status: {'CORRECTED': 45, 'CONFIRMED': 615, 'ADDED_BY_VERIFIER': 113}
- by clause group: {'timing': 142, 'annexk': 99, 'sequencing': 168, 'dctypes': 77, 'transport': 115, 'examples': 79, 'responsecodes': 93}
- cost: 18 agents, 3,680,377 tokens, 504 tool calls

`ADDED_BY_VERIFIER` = a rule the assigned extractor missed and the verifier found on re-reading; `CORRECTED` = the extractor had it but with a wrong value, party or paraphrase.

| ELEC-EVSECURRENTREGULATIONTOLERANCE | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical | EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance | unit A (Ampere), min 0, max 400; optional (minOccurs=0) | CORRECTED | EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance is an OPTIONAL element giving the absolute magnitude of the regulation tolerance of the EVSA. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'A' (Ampere), minimum 0, maximum 400. ISO 15118-2 does not define the abbreviation EVSA anywhere; it occurs only in this one table cell. |
| ELEC-EVSEENERGYTOBEDELIVERED | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit Wh (Watt-hour), min 0, max 200000; optional | CONFIRMED | EVSEEnergyToBeDelivered is an OPTIONAL element giving the amount of energy to be delivered by the EVSE. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'Wh' (Watt-hour) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 200000. |
| ELEC-EVSEMAXIMUMCURRENTLIMIT | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit A (Ampere), min 0, max 400 | CONFIRMED | EVSEMaximumCurrentLimit is the maximum current the EVSE can deliver. Per Table 68 [V2G2-832] it is expressed with unitSymbolType 'A' (Ampere) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 400. It also appears as an optional element of CurrentDemandRes where it may be updated during the charging loop. |
| ELEC-EVSEMAXIMUMPOWERLIMIT | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit W (Watt), min 0, max 200000 | CONFIRMED | EVSEMaximumPowerLimit is the maximum power the EVSE can deliver. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'W' (Watt) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 200000. |
| ELEC-EVSEMAXIMUMVOLTAGELIMIT | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit V (Volt), min 0, max 1000 | CONFIRMED | EVSEMaximumVoltageLimit is the maximum voltage the EVSE can deliver. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'V' (Volt) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 1000. |
| ELEC-EVSEMINIMUMCURRENTLIMIT | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit A (Ampere), min 0, max 400 | CONFIRMED | EVSEMinimumCurrentLimit is the minimum current the EVSE can deliver WITH THE EXPECTED ACCURACY - i.e. below this current the station's regulation accuracy is no longer guaranteed. | ELEC-EVSEMINIMUMVOLTAGELIMIT | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical |  | unit V (Volt), min 0, max 1000 | CONFIRMED | EVSEMinimumVoltageLimit is the minimum voltage the EVSE can deliver with the expected accuracy. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'V' (Volt) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 1000. |
| ELEC-EVSEPEAKCURRENTRIPPLE | V2G2-373, V2G2-832 | electrical | EVSEPeakCurrentRipple | unit A (Ampere), min 0, max 400; peak-to-peak, so half-amplitude = val | CONFIRMED | EVSEPeakCurrentRipple is a MANDATORY element giving the peak-to-peak magnitude of the current ripple of the EVSE. Per Table 68 it uses unitSymbolType 'A' (Ampere) with minimum value 0 and maximum value 400. Being peak-to-peak, the corresponding one-sided excursion about the mean is half this value. |

## clause group: sequencing  (168 rules)

| CONN-DLINK-PAUSE-ON-CHARGINGSESSION-PAUSE | V2G2-718, V2G2-725 | connection |  | Pause | CONFIRMED | If SessionStopReq carried ChargingSession = 'Pause', the EVCC pauses the data link (D-LINK_PAUSE.request) after receiving SessionStopRes and continues per [V2G2-014]; the SECC pauses after sending SessionStopRes and continues per [V2G2-721]. |
| CONN-DLINK-TERMINATE-ON-CHARGINGSESSION-TERMINATE | V2G2-717, V2G2-724 | connection |  | Terminate | CONFIRMED | If SessionStopReq carried ChargingSession = 'Terminate', the EVCC terminates the data link (D-LINK_TERMINATE.request) after receiving SessionStopRes and the SECC terminates it after sending SessionStopRes. |
| CONN-EVCC-TERMINATE-AFTER-ERROR | V2G2-728, V2G2-025 | connection |  |  | CONFIRMED | After the EVCC stopped the V2G Communication Session with an error it shall terminate the communication by applying [V2G2-025] (terminate the TLS or TCP connection). |
| CONN-EVCC-TERMINATE-AFTER-SESSIONSTOPRES | V2G2-508, V2G2-025 | connection |  |  | CONFIRMED | After receiving SessionStopRes the EVCC shall terminate the communication by applying [V2G2-025], i.e. terminate the TLS or TCP connection after stopping the V2G Communication Session. |

| RC-OK-ON-SUCCESS | V2G2-457 | enum |  | OK | CONFIRMED | A response message shall carry ResponseCode 'OK' if processing of the request was successful; where a more specific positive ResponseCode is defined for that situation, that specific code shall be used instead. |

| TIMING-EVCC-STOP-SERVICEDETAIL | V2G2-491 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout(ServiceDetailReq) / ServiceDetailRes | 5 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC shall stop the session when V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timer reaches V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout for ServiceDetailRes (Table 109: 5 s) or ServiceDetailRes carries ResponseCode = FAILED. |
| TIMING-EVCC-STOP-SERVICEDISCOVERY | V2G2-488 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout(ServiceDiscoveryReq) / ServiceDiscoveryRes | 2 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC shall stop the session when V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timer reaches V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout for ServiceDiscoveryRes (Table 109: 2 s) or the ServiceDiscoveryRes carries ResponseCode = FAILED. |
| TIMING-EVCC-STOP-SESSIONSETUP | V2G2-486, V2G2-448 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout(SessionSetupReq) / SessionSetupRes | 2 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC shall stop the V2G Communication Session when V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timer reaches V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout for SessionSetupRes (Table 109: 2 s) or the SessionSetupRes carries ResponseCode = FAILED. |
| TIMING-EVCC-STOP-SESSIONSTOP | V2G2-507 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout(SessionStopReq) / SessionStopRes | 2 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC shall stop the session when V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timer reaches V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout for SessionStopRes (Table 109: 2 s) or the response carries ResponseCode = FAILED. |
| TIMING-ONGOING-TIMEOUT-60 | V2G2-710, V2G2-711 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout | 60 s | CONFIRMED | When a response first arrives with EVSEProcessing = 'Ongoing' the EVCC starts V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timer and shall stop the V2G Communication Session if it reaches V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout (Table 109: 60 s) without receiving EVSEProcessing = 'Finished'. |
| TIMING-SECC-INITIAL-WAIT-SAP | V2G2-536 | timeout | V2G_SECC_Sequence_Timeout / Initial | 60 s | CONFIRMED | The SECC shall enter the wait state for supportedAppProtocolReq, set V2G_SECC_Sequence_Timeout to the Table 109 value (60 s), reset V2G_SECC_Sequence_Timer and start monitoring it - because no response has yet been sent to start the timer. |
| TIMING-SECC-ONGOING-PERFORMANCE-55 | V2G2-712, V2G2-713 | timeout | V2G_SECC_Ongoing_Performance_Time | 55 s | CONFIRMED | When the SECC first sends EVSEProcessing = 'Ongoing' it starts V2G_SECC_Ongoing_Timer; if that timer reaches V2G_SECC_Ongoing_Performance_Time (Table 109: 55 s) without ever sending EVSEProcessing = 'Finished', the SECC shall try to send ResponseCode = FAILED and shall stop the V2G Communication Session. |

| TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-BOUNDS-IPASSIGNMENT | V2G2-017, V2G2-014, V2G2-016 | connection | V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout | 20 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC must stop the IP address assignment mechanism of 7.6.3.2/7.6.3.3 once V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timer reaches or exceeds V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout (20 s). |
| TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-BOUNDS-SDP | V2G2-020, V2G2-018, V2G2-019 | connection | V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout | 20 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC must stop the SECC Discovery Protocol once V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timer reaches or exceeds V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout (20 s). |
| TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-BOUNDS-TCPTLS | V2G2-023, V2G2-021, V2G2-022, V2G2-646 | connection | V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout | 20 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC must stop attempting to establish the TLS or TCP connection once V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timer reaches or exceeds V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout (20 s). |
| TIMING-SETUP-SECC-BOUNDS-SDP-IP-TCP | V2G2-723, V2G2-029, V2G2-032, V2G2-030, V2G2-031, V2G2-722 | connection | V2G_SECC_CommunicationSetup_Performance_Time | 18 s | CONFIRMED | The SECC must stop the SDP server, stop the IP address assignment mechanism, and stop waiting for the TLS/TCP connection once V2G_SECC_CommunicationSetup_Timer reaches or exceeds V2G_SECC_CommunicationSetup_Performance_Time (18 s). |

| CP-AC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-3S | V2G2-860 | electrical |  | 3 s | CONFIRMED | If no error is detected, the SECC must close the contactor no later than 3 s after measuring control pilot State C or D. |
| CP-AC-EVSE-PWM-100-NO-ENERGY | V2G2-851 | electrical |  | 100 % duty cycle | CONFIRMED | If no energy is available the EVSE must apply a duty cycle of 100 %. In that case it may still advertise the availability time in the SASchedule of ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes, letting the EV begin with PowerDeliveryReq(ChargeProgress='Stop') to pause. |
| CP-AC-EVSE-PWM-AFTER-AUTHORIZATIONRES | V2G2-930, V2G2-931 | electrical |  | 5 % or nominal duty cycle | CONFIRMED | The EVSE must signal a PWM of 5 % or a nominal duty cycle after sending AuthorizationRes, and the EV must have measured a 5 % or nominal duty cycle before sending ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq. |
| CP-AC-EVSE-PWM-EIM-AUTH | V2G2-852, V2G2-853, V2G2-857 | electrical |  | 5 % / nominal duty cycle | CONFIRMED | Without positive EIM authorization information the EVSE must apply PWM = 5 %; on receiving positive EIM information it must apply a nominal duty cycle. If positive authorization applies ([V2G2-856]) and the EVSE PWM is 5 %, the EVSE must not change the PWM value until the end of the V2G communication session. |
| CP-AC-POWERDELIVERYRES-FAILED-CONTACTORERROR-START | V2G2-862 | electrical | V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time | 4.5 s | CONFIRMED | The SECC must answer PowerDeliveryRes with ResponseCode='FAILED_ContactorError' if V2G_SECC_Msg_Timer reaches or exceeds the PowerDelivery message performance time (4,5 s) and the contactor is still measured open. |
| CP-AC-POWERDELIVERYRES-FAILED-CONTACTORERROR-STOP | V2G2-865 | electrical | V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time | 4.5 s | CONFIRMED | The SECC must answer PowerDeliveryRes with ResponseCode='FAILED_ContactorError' if V2G_SECC_Msg_Timer reaches or exceeds the PowerDelivery message performance time (4,5 s) and the contactor is still measured closed after a Stop request. |
| CP-AC-POWERDELIVERYRES-OK-CONTACTOR-CLOSED | V2G2-861 | electrical | V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time | 4.5 s | CONFIRMED | If the SECC measures a closed contactor it must answer PowerDeliveryRes with ResponseCode='OK' within V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time (4,5 s for PowerDelivery per Table 109). |
| CP-AC-POWERDELIVERYRES-OK-CONTACTOR-OPEN | V2G2-864 | electrical | V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time | 4.5 s | CONFIRMED | If the SECC measures an open contactor after a Stop request it must answer PowerDeliveryRes with ResponseCode='OK' within V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time (4,5 s). |
| CP-AC-PWM-100-AFTER-CONTACTORERROR | V2G2-866 | electrical |  | 0.25 s | CONFIRMED | If either contactor-error case ([V2G2-862] or [V2G2-865]) applies, the SECC must set the PWM to 100 % no later than 250 ms after sending the PowerDeliveryRes. |

| CP-DC-CABLECHECKRES-FAILED-NO-CP-CD | V2G2-918 | electrical | V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time | 1.5 s | CONFIRMED | The SECC must answer CableCheckRes with ResponseCode='FAILED' within V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time (1,5 s) if it measures no control pilot State C or D. |

| TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-COMMSETUP-TIMEOUT-20S | V2G2-605, V2G2-446, V2G2-448 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout | 20 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC's timeout from a successful data-link establishment (D-LINK_READY.indication with DLINKSTATUS=Link established) to reception of SessionSetupRes is 20 s (Table 111). | TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-PRECHARGE-TIMEOUT-7S | V2G2-605, V2G2-704, V2G2-706 | timeout | V2G_EVCC_PreCharge_Timeout | 7 s | CONFIRMED | The EVCC's timeout for the whole pre-charge phase - first PreChargeReq of the charging session until the EV determines that the EVSE output voltage measured inside the EV has been adjusted sufficiently to the EV RESS voltage - is 7 s (Table 111). |

# Mapping onto our telemetry

Three independent passes turned the rule book into an implementable design, one per lens. What we shipped is a subset — see `models/iso_rules.py` for which hard rules survived contact with the fleet's actual behaviour.


## lens: timing

### proposed features (29)

- **pending_req_age_ratio** (high) — Live age of the oldest unanswered V2G request, as a fraction of that request type's V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout (Table 109). This is the single most valuable timing feature and the only one that grows *during* a stall instead of at the (possibly never-arriving) response.
  - compute: Tracker state: pend = {} mapping base name -> t_req, where base = msg without the trailing 'Req'/'Res'. On a v2g row ending in 'Req': pend[base] = t. On a v2g row ending in 'Res': pend.pop(base). On a new dialog (msg == 'supportedAppProtocolReq', or msg == 'SessionSetupReq' while last_res_msg != 'supportedAppProtocolRes') clear pend first. TIMEOUT = {CurrentDemand: 0.25, PowerDelivery: 5.0, ServiceDetail/PaymentDetails/CertificateInstallation/CertificateUpdate: 5.0, everything else: 2.0}. On EVERY event (v2g, hpav, tcp, sdp alike): value = max over pend of (t - t_req)/TIMEOUT[base], or 0.0 when pend is empty. Because the HomePlug LINK_STATUS poll runs at ~8 Hz (measured: 15.6 events/s during the CurrentDemand loop, p05 7.0/s), the ratio ticks upward roughly every 60-120 ms even while the V2G dialog is frozen.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 on the overwhelming majority of events. Measured healthy Req->Res latency across 387 sessions: p50 9 ms, p99.9 22 ms, max 42 ms for every message type; CurrentDemand max 36 ms. Peak clean value is therefore ~0.14 (CurrentDemand) and ~0.02 for the 2 s types. Only 1 unanswered request in 387 sessions.
  - FP risk: An unanswered request at the last row of a ring file is indistinguishable from a stall by this feature alone (idle == zero packets on this fleet). Always pair it with pending_req_overdue_liveness. Also, the dialog-reset on supportedAppProtocolReq must be applied or a retry dialog inherits the previous dialog's orphaned pending request.
- **pending_req_age_ratio_max** (high) — Worst-so-far value of pending_req_age_ratio inside the current V2G dialog: a monotone record of how close this dialog ever came to (or how far past) an EVCC message timeout, retained after the stall resolves.
  - compute: m = max(m, pending_req_age_ratio) evaluated on every event; reset m = 0.0 on a new dialog (see pending_req_age_ratio). Note this is deliberately NOT the same as the max of the *answered* latency ratio: a request that is answered at 3 s and one that is never answered both leave a high watermark here, which is what a session-level classifier wants.
  - clean looks like: <= 0.15 (bounded by the 36 ms worst observed CurrentDemand latency against its 0.25 s timeout).
  - FP risk: Same ring-seam caveat as pending_req_age_ratio; a session whose capture ends mid-request keeps a large high-water mark for the rest of the (short) remaining stream.
- **pending_req_overdue_liveness** (high) — Evidence that the capture (and the PLC link) is demonstrably still delivering packets while a request is overdue. This is the anti-ring-seam guard that separates 'the peer went silent' from 'the capture stopped'. Not a fault signal on its own; it is the qualifier that makes every unanswered-request rule safe on this fleet.
  - compute: For the oldest outstanding request R (base b, deadline d = t_req + TIMEOUT[b]): once t > d, increment n_after on every event and maintain g_after = max inter-event gap observed since d (track prev_t). value = min(n_after/20.0, 1.0) if g_after <= 1.0 else 0.0. Reset n_after=0, g_after=0 when R is answered or popped, or on a new dialog. Cheap: at most one or two outstanding requests exist at any time.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 (nothing is ever overdue in a clean session). During a real EVSE-side stall it reaches 1.0 within ~2-3 s, because LINK_STATUS.REQ/.CNF keep flowing (measured max inter-event gap inside the CurrentDemand loop: 0.51 s over 1.76 M intervals).
  - FP risk: Reads 0.0 for a genuine PLC link loss, because LINK_STATUS stops too - so it under-reports the most severe fault class. It must gate hard rules, never suppress features. A pathological charger that keeps polling the link but never answers V2G is exactly what it is designed to catch.
- **msg_latency_ratio** (medium) — Answered Req->Res latency divided by that request's V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout: 1.0 means the EVCC was entitled to abort the session at exactly this response.
  - compute: On a v2g row ending in 'Res' with base b: t_req = pend.pop(b); if present, lat = t - t_req; value = lat / TIMEOUT[b] (table as in pending_req_age_ratio). 0.0 on every other event. If pend has no entry for b, emit 0.0 and increment n_res_unpaired (see late_res_after_timeout).
  - clean looks like: p50 0.004 (9 ms / 2 s) for the 2 s types, p50 0.036 for CurrentDemand; observed clean max 0.144.
  - FP risk: Very low, but the dynamic range on this firmware is tiny (never above 0.15 in clean data), so a learned model will get almost no signal from it - it earns its place as the audit trail for the hard rules, not as a discriminator.
- **n_msg_timeout_exceed** (medium) — Count of answered request/response pairs in this dialog whose latency reached or exceeded the EVCC message timeout - i.e. responses the EVCC should have stopped waiting for, and whose acceptance is itself an EVCC conformance defect.
  - compute: On each answered pair (see msg_latency_ratio): if lat >= TIMEOUT[b], n += 1. Reset on a new dialog. Emit n on every event.
  - clean looks like: 0 (zero occurrences in 2.1 M measured pairs).
  - FP risk: None observed on clean fleet data. The only realistic trigger is a mis-paired Req/Res across a capture seam, which the dialog reset largely removes.
- **secc_perf_ratio** (medium) — Answered Req->Res latency divided by V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time for that response (Table 109). Per clause 8.7.1 this is a performance criterion, not an error condition - it belongs in features and must never drive an alarm.
  - compute: On an answered pair with response name m: perf = RES_PERFORMANCE_S[m] = {CurrentDemandRes: 0.025, ServiceDetailRes/PaymentDetailsRes/PowerDeliveryRes/Certificate*Res: 4.5, all other Res: 1.5}; value = lat/perf. 0.0 on other events.
  - clean looks like: ~0.006 for the 1.5 s types (9 ms/1.5 s). For CurrentDemandRes the clean distribution is p50 0.36, p95 0.64, p99 0.84, max 1.44 - the only message type on this fleet where the standard's performance budget has real dynamic range.
  - FP risk: Interpreting a value above 1.0 as a fault. Real chargers breach performance times routinely (the standard says so explicitly), and 40% of observed pre-charge phases already exceed the SECC pre-charge performance time while the session succeeds.
- **cd_perf_ratio_ewma** (high) — Exponentially-weighted mean of CurrentDemandRes latency measured against the standard's 25 ms SECC performance time - the health metric for the DC closed-loop controller, and the quantity the rule book calls 'the single most useful latency metric on DC captures'.
  - compute: On each answered CurrentDemand pair with latency lat: x = lat/0.025; fast: e_f += 0.2*(x - e_f) (e_f initialised to the first x); emit e_f on every event (held between CurrentDemandRes arrivals). Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: ~0.36 (9 ms median against the 25 ms budget), stable across the whole charging loop.
  - FP risk: Station-to-station firmware differences shift the baseline; use together with cd_perf_trend, which is scale-free. A charger under heavy dual-connector load may run a higher but perfectly stable mean.
- **cd_perf_trend** (high) — Scale-free rising-latency detector for the DC control loop: the rule book states that a rising CurrentDemandRes latency trend precedes most stall/abort faults, and it is a leading indicator that fires long before any timeout.
  - compute: Maintain a second, slow EWMA over the same x = lat/0.025 stream: e_s += 0.02*(x - e_s). Emit value = e_f/max(e_s, 1e-6) - 1.0 on every event, but only after at least 50 CurrentDemand pairs have been seen (emit 0.0 before that, so the loop's first seconds cannot produce a spurious trend). Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: ~0.0 (fluctuating in roughly +/-0.2); the clean latency distribution is stationary.
  - FP risk: Load-sharing hand-offs and SOC-taper transitions can nudge the controller's response time; a positive trend alone is not a fault, only a precursor. Also noisy for very short charging loops, which the 50-sample warm-up mitigates.
- **frac_cd_perf_exceed** (medium) — Fraction of CurrentDemandRes messages so far that missed the 25 ms SECC performance time. Length-normalised, so a 40-minute charge and a 2-minute one are comparable.
  - compute: n_cd += 1 on every answered CurrentDemand pair; n_slow += 1 when lat > 0.025. value = n_slow/max(n_cd,1), emitted on every event. Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: ~0.005-0.02 (clean p99 latency 21 ms, max 36 ms, so only the extreme tail crosses 25 ms).
  - FP risk: Firmware-dependent baseline; a station whose median sits at 20 ms would read ~0.4 without being faulty. Use as a per-station-normalised covariate rather than an absolute threshold.
- **seq_gap_ratio** (high) — Live time since the last response was seen, divided by V2G_EVCC_Sequence_Performance_Time (40 s) - the EV-side stall metric. It is deliberately kept apart from pending_req_age_ratio: this one blames the EVCC (it owes the next request), that one blames the SECC (it owes a response).
  - compute: On a v2g 'Res' row: last_res_t = t, last_res_msg = msg, seq_armed = True. On a v2g 'Req' row: if last_res_t is not None, gap = t - last_res_t; if gap >= 40.0 and seq_armed: n_seq_exceed += 1, seq_armed = False; then last_res_t = None. On EVERY event: value = (t - last_res_t)/40.0 if last_res_t is not None else 0.0. Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: p50 0.003 (0.134 s), p99.9 0.022 (0.893 s). In 2.11 M measured Res->Req gaps only two exceeded 40 s, and both preceded a supportedAppProtocolReq, i.e. both were dialog restarts rather than in-dialog stalls.
  - FP risk: The tail of a session (PowerDeliveryRes for a stop -> WeldingDetection -> SessionStop) legitimately contains multi-second pauses; and a capture seam produces a large gap with no packets in it. Gate with seq_gap_liveness and exclude gaps terminated by a dialog opener.
- **seq_gap_ratio_max** (medium) — Worst Res->Req gap so far in this dialog, as a fraction of the 40 s EVCC sequence performance time.
  - compute: On every event: m = max(m, seq_gap_ratio). Reset on a new dialog. (Taking the max of the live value rather than only of completed gaps means an ongoing stall is already reflected.)
  - clean looks like: <= 0.03 for essentially every clean session.
  - FP risk: Inherits the capture-seam risk of seq_gap_ratio and, being a high-water mark, keeps it for the rest of the session.
- **seq_gap_liveness** (medium) — Density guard for the currently open Res->Req gap: how densely the capture is populated while the EV is silent. Distinguishes an EV that stalled on a live link from a hole in the ring buffer.
  - compute: While last_res_t is not None: n_gap += 1 per event and g_gap = max inter-event gap since last_res_t. value = min(n_gap/50.0, 1.0) if g_gap <= 2.0 else 0.0. Reset both when the next Req arrives or on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: Near 0 in normal traffic simply because the gap closes within ~0.1 s (few events fit in it); it only climbs when a gap actually persists.
  - FP risk: Same blind spot as pending_req_overdue_liveness: a total PLC drop stops LINK_STATUS too and scores 0.
- **ongoing_ratio_machine** (high) — Elapsed time of the current EVSEProcessing='Ongoing' run on a MACHINE phase (CableCheckRes or ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes), divided by V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout (60 s). Anchored at the FIRST Ongoing of the run, as [V2G2-710] requires - a detector that re-anchors on every poll never fires.
  - compute: On a v2g 'Res' row whose msg is CableCheckRes or ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes: if evse_processing == 'Ongoing' and ong_m_t0 is None: ong_m_t0 = t; elif evse_processing == 'Finished': ong_m_t0 = None. On EVERY event: value = (t - ong_m_t0)/60.0 if ong_m_t0 is not None else 0.0. Reset on a new dialog. (EVSEProcessing is well populated on this firmware: 7873 CableCheckRes 'Ongoing' vs 75 'Finished' in a 114-session sample.)
  - clean looks like: p50 0.015 (0.9 s), p95 0.24 (14.6 s), max 0.45 (27.0 s) over 513 measured Ongoing runs.
  - FP risk: On this fleet the machine-phase Ongoing run is dominated by CableCheck, so this feature is highly correlated with cablecheck_ratio - do not double-count them in a linear model. A firmware that omits EVSEProcessing would silently pin this at 0.
- **ongoing_ratio_auth** (medium) — The same 60 s Ongoing clock, but for ContractAuthenticationRes only, kept as a SEPARATE feature because an authorization Ongoing loop usually means the driver has not presented their card yet - human latency, not equipment failure.
  - compute: Identical to ongoing_ratio_machine with a separate anchor ong_a_t0 driven only by ContractAuthenticationRes rows (Ongoing sets, Finished clears). Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 in nearly all sessions (only 4 ContractAuthenticationRes 'Ongoing' rows against 78 'Finished' in a 114-session sample - EIM authorization on this fleet is effectively instant).
  - FP risk: This is the false-positive containment feature: ISO's Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction does not exist in the DIN enumeration, so a DIN charger waiting for the customer emits plain 'Ongoing' and is indistinguishable from a stalled backend. Never promote it to a hard rule.
- **ongoing_secc_escape_missed** (medium) — The SECC's own escape hatch failed: an Ongoing run has passed V2G_SECC_Ongoing_Performance_Time (55 s) without the SECC sending a FAILED ResponseCode, so the charger has left the vehicle to time out at 60 s instead of failing the session cleanly. Applies to machine phases only.
  - compute: value = 1.0 if ong_m_t0 is not None and (t - ong_m_t0) >= 55.0 and no v2g row with resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED') has been seen since ong_m_t0, else 0.0. Evaluated on every event.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 (clean Ongoing runs max out at 27 s).
  - FP risk: DIN 70121 publishes its own timer table and the 55 s figure is transcribed from ISO; a DIN charger conforming to a different value would be scored non-conformant. Because the clean maximum is 27 s the margin is large enough that this is theoretical rather than practical.
- **cablecheck_ratio** (high) — Live elapsed time of the cable-check (isolation) phase divided by V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout (40 s), anchored at the FIRST CableCheckReq of the dialog per [V2G2-700]. The dominant DC phase-duration fault on real captures.
  - compute: On the first v2g 'CableCheckReq' of the dialog: cc_t0 = t. Completion cc_end = t of the first CableCheckRes with resp_code not starting with FAILED and evse_processing == 'Finished'; fall back to the first PreChargeReq after cc_t0 (per [V2G2-525] a PreChargeReq is itself proof the cable check finished) for firmware that omits EVSEProcessing. value = ((cc_end or t) - cc_t0)/40.0, 0.0 when cc_t0 is None. Once cc_end is set the value FREEZES - it is a record of how close that cable check came to the limit, not a clock that keeps running through the charge. Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: p50 0.37 (14.8 s), p95 0.43 (17.1 s), max 0.68 (27.0 s) over 254 measured cable checks; zero clean cable checks exceeded 38 s or 40 s.
  - FP risk: A ring file that resumes inside a cable-check loop has no observable cc_t0; the anchor must be a CableCheckReq actually seen after a dialog opener, otherwise a mid-phase capture start would fabricate a short phase (harmless) and a mid-phase capture *end* leaves the clock running (guard with the density check used by the hard rule).
- **cablecheck_perf_exceed** (medium) — The SECC missed its own 38 s cable-check performance time (2 s inside the vehicle's 40 s timeout) - the warning band that precedes an EV-side abort.
  - compute: value = 1.0 if cc_t0 is not None and cc_end is None and (t - cc_t0) >= 38.0 else 0.0, evaluated on every event.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 (no clean cable check reached 38 s).
  - FP risk: The DIN cable-check performance value is not necessarily 38 s; treated as a warning feature only, never a rule.
- **cablecheck_poll_count** (low) — Number of CableCheckReq polls issued in the current cable-check phase. Reported alongside the elapsed ratio because the rule book asks for the count as well as the duration when scoring a stuck isolation test.
  - compute: n_cc += 1 on each v2g 'CableCheckReq' after cc_t0 (including the first). Emit n_cc on every event; freeze at cc_end; reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: Roughly 100-130 polls (the loop runs at ~7 Hz for ~15 s); a stalled loop keeps climbing past that with no Finished.
  - FP risk: Poll cadence is firmware-specific, so the count alone does not say the phase is long - always read with cablecheck_ratio.
- **precharge_ratio** (high) — Live elapsed time of the pre-charge phase divided by V2G_EVCC_PreCharge_Timeout (7 s), anchored at the first PreChargeReq of the dialog per [V2G2-704].
  - compute: On the first v2g 'PreChargeReq' of the dialog: pc_t0 = t. Completion: the first PowerDeliveryReq after pc_t0 (pre-charge finishing is an EV-internal voltage judgement with no protocol flag, so the transition to PowerDelivery is the only observable end marker). value = ((pc_end or t) - pc_t0)/7.0, frozen once pc_end is set; 0.0 when pc_t0 is None. Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: p50 0.67 (4.7 s), p95 0.97 (6.8 s), max 1.26 (8.85 s) over 229 measured pre-charges. NOTE 3% of clean pre-charges exceed the ISO 7 s timeout.
  - FP risk: High at ratio 1.0: because 7/229 clean sessions exceeded 7 s, the standard's own timeout is NOT a safe alarm point for this fleet - either the DIN pre-charge value differs from ISO's or these chargers are routinely marginal. Keep the ratio as a feature and set any rule threshold above the observed clean maximum.
- **precharge_perf_ratio** (medium) — The same pre-charge span expressed against the SECC's 5 s performance time, i.e. how far the charger is into its 'slow but legal' band while ramping its output to the pack voltage.
  - compute: value = ((pc_end or t) - pc_t0)/5.0 when pc_t0 is set, else 0.0; frozen at pc_end; reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: p50 0.94, and 40% of clean pre-charges (92/229) exceed 1.0.
  - FP risk: Very high if thresholded: on this fleet exceeding the SECC pre-charge performance time is the norm, not an anomaly. This feature is a graded covariate for the model and explicitly disqualified from any rule.
- **comm_setup_ratio** (high) — Live time from the observable proxy for data-link readiness to SessionSetupRes, divided by V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout (20 s). Covers address assignment, SDP, TCP establishment and the SessionSetup exchange in one budget.
  - compute: setup_t0 = the earliest of: an hpav row containing 'SLAC_MATCH.CNF' (or 'CM_SET_KEY.CNF'), the first sdp row, the first tcp row, or the first v2g row of the dialog - whichever occurs first after the dialog opens. setup_res_t = t of the first SessionSetupRes. value = ((setup_res_t or t) - setup_t0)/20.0, frozen once SessionSetupRes arrives; 0.0 while setup_t0 is None or comm_setup_anchor_valid == 0. Reset on a new dialog.
  - clean looks like: Well under 0.2 whenever the dialog opener is in the capture; SLAC-to-SessionSetupRes on this fleet is a couple of seconds at most.
  - FP risk: setup_t0 is a LOWER bound on the true D-LINK_READY moment (link-up is not in the capture), so the measured span understates the real one - the error is in the safe direction for detecting a breach, but it means the feature cannot be used to certify that setup was fast. A capture that starts mid-charge has no setup to time at all, which is why the validity flag exists.
- **comm_setup_anchor_valid** (medium) — Whether this session actually contains the opening of a V2G dialog, so that setup, cable-check and sequence timers are being measured from a real anchor rather than from a ring-buffer seam.
  - compute: value = 1.0 once a dialog opener has been observed in this session (a v2g 'supportedAppProtocolReq', or a 'SessionSetupReq' not preceded by a supportedAppProtocolRes, or an hpav SLAC_MATCH.CNF), else 0.0. Set once, never cleared within a dialog.
  - clean looks like: 1.0 for sessions captured from the plug-in; 0.0 for the (common) ring files that resume mid-charge.
  - FP risk: None; this is a validity flag. Its main job is to prevent the setup and cable-check rules from firing on seam sessions.
- **sessionstop_pending_ratio** (high) — Age of an unanswered SessionStopReq divided by its 2 s message timeout. The cleanest early marker of an unclean session end - it fires while the stream is still alive, instead of waiting for the stream to die.
  - compute: On a v2g 'SessionStopReq': ss_t = t. On 'SessionStopRes': ss_t = None. On every event: value = (t - ss_t)/2.0 if ss_t is not None else 0.0.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 apart from a sub-0.02 blip; measured SessionStop latency p50 9 ms, max 21 ms, with 1 unanswered SessionStopReq across 387 sessions.
  - FP risk: A capture that ends between SessionStopReq and SessionStopRes (a plausible ring-seam position, since the link goes idle right after) would leave this high - gate the corresponding rule on liveness.
- **powerdelivery_pending_ratio** (high) — Age of an unanswered PowerDeliveryReq divided by its 5 s timeout - the contactor-actuation window. The long ISO budget exists precisely because the SECC must operate and verify the contactor before answering, so a breach here points at hardware, not comms.
  - compute: On a v2g 'PowerDeliveryReq': pd_t = t (record extra.ReadyToChargeState if present, to distinguish the start transition from the stop transition). On 'PowerDeliveryRes': pd_t = None. On every event: value = (t - pd_t)/5.0 if pd_t is not None else 0.0.
  - clean looks like: 0.0; measured PowerDelivery latency p50 8 ms, max 22 ms over 541 pairs.
  - FP risk: Low. Note DIN 70121 carries no FAILED_ContactorError, so a stuck contactor here shows up as a long silence or a bare FAILED rather than a diagnostic code - the timing feature is the primary evidence, not a corroborator.
- **tail_stall_ratio** (medium) — Time spent after the PowerDeliveryRes that answered the stop request without the mandated WeldingDetectionReq or SessionStopReq following, as a fraction of the 40 s sequence performance time. Catches sessions that die during the shutdown handshake.
  - compute: Set tail_t0 = t on the PowerDeliveryRes that answers a PowerDeliveryReq whose extra.ReadyToChargeState is false/0 (DIN's stop marker; ChargeProgress is absent from this firmware's extra field). Clear tail_t0 on the next WeldingDetectionReq or SessionStopReq. value = (t - tail_t0)/40.0 if tail_t0 is not None else 0.0, evaluated on every event.
  - clean looks like: Small but nonzero - the closing handshake legitimately pauses for seconds (the current labeller already carves it out of its freeze scan). Expect < 0.2.
  - FP risk: If extra.ReadyToChargeState is absent the stop transition cannot be identified and the feature stays 0; fall back to 'the last PowerDeliveryRes seen after the CurrentDemand loop ended'. Sessions that end here are also the most likely to be cut by a ring seam, since the link goes idle immediately after.
- **n_res_unpaired** (low) — Count of response messages that did not match the outstanding request - the [V2G2-482] pairing invariant. A mismatch obliges the EVCC to stop the session, so it is both a sequencing fault and an explanation for a subsequent abrupt teardown.
  - compute: On a v2g 'Res' row whose base is not in pend: n += 1 (do not update last_res_t bookkeeping differently; still set last_res_msg/last_res_t so the sequence timer stays sane). Reset on a new dialog. Emit n on every event.
  - clean looks like: 0. A non-zero value in practice usually means a capture seam split a Req/Res pair rather than a genuine protocol violation.
  - FP risk: High on ring-seam sessions, where the Req was captured in the previous file. Suppress entirely when comm_setup_anchor_valid == 0.
- **n_redialog** (medium) — Number of extra V2G dialogs opened inside one session. The standard defines no restart transition, so the only route back to supportedAppProtocolReq/SessionSetupReq is a terminated session - a second dialog is proof the first one died, and the count is a severity measure for a retry storm.
  - compute: n_dialog += 1 on each dialog opener (supportedAppProtocolReq, or SessionSetupReq not preceded by supportedAppProtocolRes). value = max(0, n_dialog - 1), emitted on every event.
  - clean looks like: 0.
  - FP risk: A SessionStopReq with ChargingSession='Pause' followed by a resume would be a legitimate second dialog - but DIN 70121's SessionStopReq carries no ChargingSession element, so pause/resume does not exist on this fleet and the interpretation is unambiguous here.
- **sdp_unanswered_burst** (low) — Consecutive SECC Discovery Requests with no discovery response, against the standard's 50-request stop condition; plus the sub-250 ms retry-interval check. Included for completeness of the communication-setup budget.
  - compute: On a kind=='sdp' row, classify the info line by substring: 'Request'/'Req' -> req, 'Response'/'Res' -> res. n_sdp_unanswered += 1 on a req, reset to 0 on a res. Also track min inter-request interval imin. value = min(n_sdp_unanswered/50.0, 1.0); secondary output 1.0 if imin < 0.25 (an over-eager client) else 0.0.
  - clean looks like: 0.0.
  - FP risk: Effectively untestable on this fleet: a 114-session sample contained ZERO kind=='sdp' rows, so the extractor is either not emitting SDP or these chargers do not use it in a way tshark surfaced. The req/res split also depends on an info-line substring that has not been verified. Treat any non-zero value as suspect until an SDP-bearing capture is found.
- **capture_gap_max_recent** (medium) — Largest inter-event gap in the last 60 s of the stream, of any kind. Not a conformance quantity - it is the context variable every timing feature needs, because on this fleet idle means zero packets and a hole in the capture is indistinguishable from silence on the wire unless you measure the surrounding density.
  - compute: Keep a deque of the last 60 s of event timestamps; on each event push t, pop while t - front > 60.0, and emit the max successive difference in the deque (O(1) amortised with a running monotonic-max structure, or recompute over the small window).
  - clean looks like: ~0.5 s during the CurrentDemand loop (measured max inter-event gap 0.51 s over 1.76 M intervals) and up to a few seconds in quieter phases.
  - FP risk: None - it is a guard, and its purpose is to stop other features from producing them.

### proposed hard rules (7)

- `cablecheck_timeout_40s` → ISOLATION_STALL (map to the existing COMM_FREEZE/ISOLATION_FAULT families; anchor the fault at cc_t0 + 40.0) (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: comm_setup_anchor_valid == 1 AND cc_t0 is not None AND cc_end is None AND (t - cc_t0) >= 40.0 AND (number of v2g rows with t in [cc_t0, t]) >= 20 AND (max inter-event gap of ANY kind within [cc_t0, t]) <= 5.0
  - why: V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout is 40 s measured from the first CableCheckReq of the charging session to a CableCheckRes with ResponseCode OK and EVSEProcessing Finished; at that point the EVCC is required to abort. Empirically the margin is enormous on this fleet: 254 clean cable checks had p50 14.8 s, p95 17.1 s and a maximum of 27.0 s, and NOT ONE reached even the SECC's 38 s performance time. A cable check still running at 40 s with the poll loop demonstrably alive is an isolation test that will not complete.
  - FP risk: Only two realistic ways to be wrong: (a) the ring file ends inside the cable-check loop, which the density guard rejects because no events exist to satisfy it; (b) DIN 70121 publishes a cable-check timeout longer than ISO's 40 s - possible in principle, but the 13 s gap between the clean maximum and the threshold makes a benign 40 s cable check very unlikely on this hardware.
- `request_unanswered_past_timeout_on_live_link` → COMM_FREEZE (SECC-side stall; anchor the fault at t_req + TIMEOUT[b], the instant the standard's own timer expires) (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: exists an outstanding request R (base b, sent at t_req, TIMEOUT[b] from Table 109) with: (t - t_req) >= max(2.0 * TIMEOUT[b], 1.5) AND b != 'SessionStop' AND (events observed after t_req + TIMEOUT[b]) >= 10 AND (max inter-event gap after t_req + TIMEOUT[b]) <= 1.0
  - why: [V2G2-436..438]: the EVCC arms V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout when it sends the request and must stop the session when the timer expires with no response. The effective thresholds are 1.5 s for CurrentDemand (6x its 0.25 s timeout), 4 s for the 2 s messages and 10 s for PowerDelivery. Measured clean Req->Res latency is p50 9 ms and max 42 ms across 2.1 M pairs, with exactly one unanswered request in 387 sessions - so the threshold sits 35x above anything healthy. The liveness guard proves the medium is still carrying packets: the HomePlug LINK_STATUS poll runs at ~15 events/s during charging and the largest clean inter-event gap measured inside a CurrentDemand loop was 0.51 s.
  - FP risk: A ring-buffer seam or an end-of-file cannot satisfy the '>=10 events with no gap above 1 s after the deadline' guard, so the classic idle-equals-zero-packets artefact is excluded by construction. The residual risk is a firmware that keeps the PLC link poll running while deliberately pausing V2G for a reason the standard permits - no such mechanism exists in DIN 70121 for the DC message set.
- `powerdelivery_unanswered_10s` → EVSE_FAULT (contactor actuation; anchor at pd_t + 5.0) (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: pd_t is not None AND (t - pd_t) >= 10.0 AND (events after pd_t + 5.0) >= 20 AND (max inter-event gap after pd_t + 5.0) <= 1.0
  - why: PowerDeliveryReq carries the long 5 s EVCC timeout / 4.5 s SECC performance time precisely because the charger must operate AND verify its contactor before answering. Silence for twice that budget while the link is alive is the DIN-dialect signature of the fault ISO would report as FAILED_ContactorError or FAILED_PowerDeliveryNotApplied - codes DIN 70121 either lacks or uses sparingly, so the timing is the primary evidence. Measured clean PowerDelivery latency: p50 8 ms, max 22 ms over 541 pairs.
  - FP risk: Overlaps request_unanswered_past_timeout_on_live_link (which would also fire at 10 s); it exists to attribute the fault family to hardware rather than to comms. Deduplicate at the session level or the same event yields two alarms.
- `sessionstop_unanswered_4s` → SESSION_ABORT (unclean close; anchor at ss_t + 2.0) (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: ss_t is not None AND (t - ss_t) >= 4.0 AND (events after ss_t + 2.0) >= 10 AND (max inter-event gap after ss_t + 2.0) <= 1.0
  - why: SessionStopReq must be answered within 2 s, and the session is only 'stopped without error' once SessionStopRes has been exchanged; a connection torn down before SessionStopRes is by the standard's own words always an error. Using twice the timeout plus a liveness guard turns the existing 'no SessionStopRes anywhere in the session' post-hoc test into an online alarm that fires while the stream is still running - which is exactly what earliness scoring rewards. Measured: SessionStop latency p50 9 ms, max 21 ms; 1 unanswered SessionStopReq in 387 sessions.
  - FP risk: The end of a session is the most likely place for a ring seam, because the PLC link goes idle immediately afterwards; the liveness guard is doing all the work here and must not be relaxed. A charger that answers at, say, 3 s would be missed - deliberate, since the 2 s figure is transcribed from ISO and DIN publishes its own table.
- `ongoing_machine_phase_over_60s` → COMM_FREEZE (processing stall; anchor at ong_m_t0 + 60.0) (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: ong_m_t0 is not None (anchored on the FIRST CableCheckRes or ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes carrying EVSEProcessing == 'Ongoing' of the run) AND no response of that phase with EVSEProcessing == 'Finished' since AND (t - ong_m_t0) >= 60.0 AND (v2g rows in [ong_m_t0, t]) >= 20 AND (max inter-event gap in [ong_m_t0, t]) <= 5.0
  - why: V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout is 60 s from the FIRST Ongoing response, and the EVCC must stop the session at that point; the SECC should already have emitted FAILED at 55 s. Restricting the rule to the two machine phases is essential: on a DIN charger an authorization Ongoing loop means the driver has not swiped yet, because DIN has no Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction value to say so. Measured clean Ongoing runs: p50 0.9 s, p95 14.6 s, max 27.0 s over 513 runs.
  - FP risk: Largely redundant with cablecheck_timeout_40s on this fleet, since CableCheckRes accounts for 7873 of 7955 observed 'Ongoing' rows - it earns its keep for the ChargeParameterDiscovery case and for firmware where the cable-check anchor is missing. If a charger omitted EVSEProcessing entirely the rule silently never fires (a miss, not a false alarm).
- `evcc_sequence_stall_40s` → COMM_FREEZE (EV-side stall; anchor at last_res_t + 40.0) (confidence 0.8)
  - condition: last_res_t is not None AND (t - last_res_t) >= 40.0 AND (events after last_res_t) >= 100 AND (max inter-event gap after last_res_t) <= 2.0 AND the gap has not been terminated by a dialog opener
  - why: Every 'after receiving X the EVCC shall send Y' requirement in 8.8.4.2 is qualified by 'while V2G_EVCC_Sequence_Timer is smaller than V2G_EVCC_Sequence_Performance_Time' (40 s), and the SECC will drop the session at 60 s. Empirically this is one of the sharpest separators available: over 2.11 M Res->Req gaps, p99.9 was 0.89 s and only two exceeded 40 s - both immediately preceding a supportedAppProtocolReq, i.e. dialog restarts rather than in-dialog stalls, which the exclusion clause removes. Note this blames the vehicle, not the charger, and is the correct home for stalls the current labeller lumps into a generic delivery freeze.
  - FP risk: Strictly a performance time, so ISO does not mandate error handling at 40 s - the justification here is empirical (2 in 2.11 M, both explained) rather than normative, which is why the confidence is below the timeout rules. The 100-event density guard is the essential protection against the ring-seam case; without it, any capture hole longer than 40 s would fire.
- `comm_setup_over_25s_no_sessionsetupres` → SLAC_FAILURE / SETUP_TIMEOUT (communication setup never completed; anchor at setup_t0 + 20.0) (confidence 0.75)
  - condition: comm_setup_anchor_valid == 1 AND setup_t0 is not None AND setup_res_t is None AND (t - setup_t0) >= 25.0 AND (events after setup_t0 + 20.0) >= 20 AND (max inter-event gap after setup_t0 + 20.0) <= 2.0
  - why: V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timeout is 20 s from data-link readiness to SessionSetupRes and covers IP assignment, SDP, TCP and the SessionSetup exchange; the SECC must itself have torn the connection down at 18 s. Because setup_t0 (first SLAC_MATCH.CNF / SDP / TCP / V2G row) is a LOWER bound on the true D-LINK_READY instant, the measured span understates the real one - so a measured breach implies a real breach. The 25 s threshold adds margin for the anchor's coarseness and for DIN publishing its own value.
  - FP risk: The anchor is the weak point: if SLAC matching for the neighbouring connector is what set setup_t0 the span is meaningless. Prefer an anchor from IP-layer evidence (first tcp or sdp row) over SLAC when both exist, since the logical PLC networks are keyed separately and IP traffic on this capture is ours. Requires comm_setup_anchor_valid, so mid-charge ring files cannot trigger it.

### ground-truth changes the standard implies (9)

- Stop measuring the delivery stall as a gap between consecutive V2G messages and measure it as the age of an unanswered request against that request type's V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout (CurrentDemand 0.25 s, most 2 s, PowerDelivery 5 s), with the fault anchored at t_req + timeout. Keep a fleet margin for the LABEL (fire at max(2xT, 1.5 s)) but move the ANCHOR to the standard's deadline.
  - today: FREEZE_S = 5.0 applied to the gap between any two v2g rows inside the CurrentDemand window, anchored at prev_t + 5 s.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-436..438]: the EVCC arms a per-message-type timer when it SENDS a request and must stop the session when that timer expires with no response. The quantity is the Req->Res pair, and the bound for CurrentDemand is 250 ms, not 5 s.
  - risk: Low, and it corrects a real measurement error. Applying 0.25 s to the WRONG quantity (message-to-message gaps) would flood: clean v2g gaps inside the loop reach 0.90 s. Applying it to the RIGHT quantity does not: clean Req->Res latency maxes at 36 ms for CurrentDemand, so the standard's bound sits 7x above anything healthy. Moving the anchor earlier does inflate measured earliness for every detector by up to 4.75 s on delivery freezes - re-baseline the earliness scores before comparing to the 2026-08-13 leaderboard.
- Split the single COMM_FREEZE family by which party owes the next message: an unanswered request past its timeout is a SECC-side stall; a Res->next-Req gap past 40 s is an EVCC-side (vehicle) stall. Label them as distinct causes and require the density guard (>=10 events after the deadline with no sub-gap above 1 s) before labelling either.
  - today: One undirected stall test over consecutive v2g rows inside the CurrentDemand window; it cannot say whether the charger or the car went quiet, and it fires on any capture hole in that window.
  - under ISO: Clause 8.7.1 defines two separate timer categories owned by two different parties: the EVCC's Msg Timer (request sent -> response awaited) and the Sequence Timer (response sent -> next request awaited, 40 s EVCC performance / 60 s SECC timeout).
  - risk: Medium. The density guard removes labels wherever the capture is sparse, so total COMM_FREEZE count will fall; some genuine faults where the PLC link died entirely (LINK_STATUS stops too) will lose their label and must be caught by the abort path instead. This is a deliberate trade of recall for a much lower false-positive rate on ring-seam artefacts.
- Lower the stuck-CableCheck threshold from 60 s to the standard's 40 s, anchor it at the first CableCheckReq OF THAT DIALOG, and define completion as a CableCheckRes with a non-FAILED ResponseCode AND EVSEProcessing == 'Finished' (falling back to the first subsequent PreChargeReq), not as 'the next PreChargeReq or PowerDeliveryReq' alone.
  - today: CABLECHECK_STUCK_S = 60.0, anchored at the first CableCheckReq in the session, completion inferred from the next PreChargeReq/PowerDeliveryReq or the last CableCheck row.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout = 40 s from the first CableCheckReq of the charging session until a CableCheckRes with ResponseCode OK and EVSEProcessing Finished; the SECC's own performance limit is 38 s.
  - risk: Low. 254 clean cable checks measured p50 14.8 s, max 27.0 s and NONE exceeded 38 s, so 40 s adds no false positives on this fleet while catching stalls in the 40-60 s band that are currently unlabelled. EVSEProcessing is well populated here (7873 Ongoing / 75 Finished on CableCheckRes), so the completion test is real rather than theoretical. The anchor change matters for sessions containing a retry dialog, where the current per-session anchor reports a fabricated multi-minute cable check.
- Add a pre-charge phase timeout as a labelled fault family, but set the label threshold at 10 s rather than at the standard's 7 s, and require that EVSEPresentVoltage failed to converge toward EVTargetVoltage over the phase.
  - today: No pre-charge timing label exists at all; a charger that cannot ramp its output to the pack voltage is only labelled if it happens to emit a FAILED code or die.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_PreCharge_Timeout = 7 s from the first PreChargeReq (SECC performance time 5 s); at expiry the EVCC applies the error handling of 8.8.
  - risk: Medium-high if the standard's 7 s were used literally: 92 of 229 clean pre-charges (40%) already exceed the 5 s SECC performance time and 7 of 229 (3%) exceed the 7 s EVCC timeout while the session goes on to charge successfully. Either DIN publishes a longer value or this hardware is routinely marginal. 10 s sits above the observed clean maximum of 8.85 s; the convergence check guards the rest. Introduces a new positive class, so retrain and re-split before comparing scores.
- Add an Ongoing-phase timeout label (60 s from the first EVSEProcessing='Ongoing' of a run) for CableCheckRes and ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes ONLY, and explicitly exclude ContractAuthenticationRes from it.
  - today: No Ongoing timer is labelled; a charge-parameter negotiation that never reaches Finished is only caught if the session also dies.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-710/711]: the EVCC starts the Ongoing timer at the FIRST Ongoing response and must stop the session at 60 s without a Finished; [V2G2-713] obliges the SECC to send FAILED at 55 s. ISO distinguishes a backend wait ('Ongoing') from a customer wait ('Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction'), but DIN's enumeration has only Ongoing/Finished.
  - risk: Low for the machine phases (clean Ongoing runs max at 27 s). The exclusion of authorization is the important half: because DIN cannot say 'waiting for the driver', a long ContractAuthentication Ongoing loop is most often a human at the kiosk, and labelling it would manufacture a fault family out of customer behaviour. On this fleet that case is rare anyway (4 Ongoing vs 78 Finished authorization responses).
- Require positive evidence before labelling SESSION_ABORT, instead of inferring it from the absence of SessionStopRes. Accept any of: an unanswered SessionStopReq past 2 s with the capture demonstrably alive; a TCP RST; an unanswered request past its message timeout with the liveness guard satisfied; or a second dialog inside the session. When none of these holds and the session simply stops at a file boundary - especially after ChargingComplete=TRUE or after EVSENotification=StopCharging followed by a stop-side PowerDelivery exchange - label it benign/unknown rather than faulty.
  - today: graceful = (a SessionStopRes exists); everything else is SESSION_ABORT, anchored at the RST or at the last row of the session.
  - under ISO: A TCP connection terminated before SessionStopRes is always an error - but that requires observing the termination. The standard also provides two legitimate routes to a silent end that are not equipment faults: the EVCC stopping on CP State E/F (unplug), and, in ISO, a pause; and per 8.7.1 the party that owes the next message is identified by which timer expired.
  - risk: High impact, and the highest-value change here. On this fleet idle means zero packets and nearly every session ends at a ring-file boundary followed by an hours-long hole, so 'no SessionStopRes' is heavily confounded with capture truncation - SESSION_ABORT is currently 624 of 789 faults (79%). Tightening it will remove a large block of positives; some genuine silent aborts (total PLC loss, where LINK_STATUS also stops) will be reclassified as unknown and recall on that family will fall. Recommend introducing an explicit UNKNOWN/censored class rather than flipping those sessions to negative, so they are excluded from both recall and false-alarm accounting.
- On a row whose resp_code starts with FAILED, do not additionally raise ISOLATION_FAULT, EVSE_FAULT or EV_ERROR from the other fields of that same row - record only PROTOCOL_FAILED. Read status, isolation and error fields from the last non-FAILED response instead.
  - today: The marker scan reads resp_code, evse_status, isolation and ev_err independently from every row, including rows carrying a FAILED ResponseCode.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-735/736]: when the SECC sends a FAILED ResponseCode the EVCC shall ignore all other parameters, and the SECC fills the mandatory fields with arbitrary XSD-conformant values chosen to minimise message size. Those numbers are placeholders by design.
  - risk: Low in count but it changes first_fault_family and hence the fault attribution and the earliness anchor for sessions that fail with a FAILED code. Since the labeller deduplicates families and keeps the earliest of each, the effect is on which family a session is credited with, not on whether it is faulty.
- Treat EVErrorCode == 'NoData' as benign before the charging loop opens, and as a fault only if it persists into CurrentDemand. Add it to BENIGN_EV_ERR with a phase condition.
  - today: BENIGN_EV_ERR = {'', 'NO_ERROR'}, so every 'NoData' row raises EV_ERROR.
  - under ISO: Table 100: NoData means the vehicle has not yet determined its operating state, and is expected in the earliest DC requests; [V2G2-881] makes all EVErrorCodes informational, so the EVSE must not react to them at all.
  - risk: Small recall loss if any current EV_ERROR labels rest solely on NoData rows (EV_ERROR is only 27 of 789 faults, so the blast radius is small). The phase condition preserves the genuinely diagnostic case of an EV that never resolves its own state.
- Bound SLAC_FAILURE by the 20 s communication-setup budget and require evidence that the matching attempt belongs to THIS connector - an EVSE-originated frame (CM_SLAC_PARM.CNF, CM_ATTEN_CHAR.RSP, CM_SET_KEY.CNF) or IP-layer activity (a tcp or sdp row) - rather than a bare count of SLAC frames.
  - today: SLAC_MIN_ROWS = 3: any three SLAC/ATTEN_CHAR frames with no V2G dialog make the chunk a SLAC_FAILURE.
  - under ISO: The SDP client stops after 50 unanswered discovery requests, and the EVCC must stop IP assignment, SDP and TCP establishment once V2G_EVCC_CommunicationSetup_Timer reaches 20 s - the failure is defined by a budget expiring, not by a frame count.
  - risk: Medium. The powerline is a shared medium and the neighbouring connector's SLAC broadcasts are captured here, which is exactly the confusion that invalidated the 2026-08-13 labels once already; the observed frame mix (CM_SLAC_PARM.REQ 107 vs CM_SLAC_PARM.CNF 82) shows both directions are present, so the EVSE-response test is usable. Risk in the other direction: a matching attempt that our EVSE never answered at all would produce no .CNF and would now go unlabelled - accept that, because such a trace is also indistinguishable from a neighbour's failed match.

### not checkable from our capture

CP-EVCC-STATE-E-F-ABORT / CP-CONFORMANCE-61851 / CP-DC-EV-STATE-CD-BEFORE-CABLECHECK / CP-DC-EV-STATE-B-AFTER-STOP / CP-DC-EV-NO-STATE-CHANGE-DURING-LOOP / CP-DC-EVSE-5PCT-DUTY-WHOLE-SESSION / CP-DC-CABLECHECK-MONITOR-CP / CP-DC-WAIT-STATE-B-PERFORMANCE-WINDOW / DC-CP-STATE-HOLD / SEQ-DC-CP-STATE-TRANSITIONS / SEQ-EVCC-CP-STATE-EF-STOP / AC-CP-STATE-250MS-DEADLINES / AC-CP-TIMING-250MS / DC-EVSE-PWM-5-PERCENT-WHOLE-SESSION / AC-EVSE-PWM-SIGNALLING / CP-AC-* (all): missing field - IEC 61851-1 control pilot state and PWM duty cycle. No analogue signalling is captured; only the message-level consequences (a FAILED CableCheckRes, a FAILED WeldingDetectionRes/SessionStopRes) are observable, never the CP state itself., NOTIF-STOPCHARGING-MAXDELAY / NOTIF-RENEGOTIATION-MAXDELAY / ENUM-STOPCHARGING-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / ENUM-RENEGOTIATION-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / ENUM-EVSENOTIFICATION-STOPCHARGING / ENUM-EVSENOTIFICATION-RENEGOTIATION / FIELD-NOTIFICATIONMAXDELAY / NOTIF-EVSENOTIFICATION-NONE / RENEG-EVSENOTIFICATION-MAXDELAY / SEQ-AC-EVSE-TRIGGERED-RENEG / RENEG-SECC-INITIATED-VIA-EVSENOTIFICATION: missing field - NotificationMaxDelay. The telemetry carries `notification` (None/StopCharging/ReNegotiation) but not the companion unsignedShort delay, so the deadline these rules define cannot be computed. Only a fixed-window approximation (time from the first StopCharging to the stop-side PowerDeliveryReq) is available, and it is a heuristic, not a conformance check., TIMING-SECC-SEQTIMER-EXPIRY-STOPS-SESSION / SEQ-SECC-SEQUENCE-TIMEOUT-60 / TIMING-SECC-SEQUENCE-TIMEOUT-60S / ABNORM-SECC-SEQUENCE-TIMEOUT / SECC-COMMSETUP-18S-ACTION / TIMING-SETUP-SECC-EXPIRY-TERMINATES / TIMING-SECC-INITIAL-WAIT-SAP / SEQ-SECC-FIRST-MESSAGE-SEQUENCE-TIMER / STATE-SECC-WAIT-CONNECTION-TIMEOUT / CONN-SECC-TERMINATE-AFTER-SESSION-STOP / CONN-SECC-CLOSE-AFTER-FAILED / CONN-EVCC-TERMINATE-AFTER-SESSIONSTOPRES / SEQ-EVCC-TERMINATE-CONNECTION-AFTER-STOP: missing field - TCP connection lifecycle. The tcp kind emits only RST / RETX / LOST; there is no SYN, SYN-ACK or FIN, so 'did the peer close the socket at 60 s / 18 s' and 'was the connection left dangling' are unobservable. Compounded by the pipeline: sessionize.py cuts a session on a 60 s activity gap, so the SECC's 60 s sequence timeout can essentially never be observed to expire inside one session - the 40 s EVCC performance band is the only sequence bound that is reachable in this data., SDP-RES-PAYLOAD-FORMAT / SDP-REQUEST-FORMAT-AND-PORTS / SDP-SECURITY-TRANSPORT-NEGOTIATION / SDP-OPTION-TCP-MIRROR / SDP-OPTION-TLS-MIRROR / SDP-OPTION-TLS-FALLBACK / SDP-UDP-PORTS / SDP-REQ-MULTICAST-DEST / SDP-RESPONSE-UNICAST-BACK-TO-CLIENT / EVCC-USES-SDP-RETURNED-TCP-PORT / V2GTP-TCP-PORTS-DYNAMIC-RANGE: missing field - SDP payload internals (payload type, length, security/transport bytes, SECC IP and port) and UDP/TCP port numbers. Worse in practice: a 114-session sample contained ZERO kind=='sdp' rows, so even the req/res timing rules (SDP-CLIENT-WAIT-250MS, SDP-CLIENT-MAX-50-REQUESTS) have no data to run on and their features will read 0 fleet-wide., V2GTP-HEADER-VERSION-PATTERN / V2GTP-PAYLOAD-TYPES / V2GTP-ONE-V2G-MSG-PER-PDU / V2GTP-HEADER-CHECK-ORDER-AND-DISCARD / V2GTP-MALFORMED-HEADER-IGNORE / EXI-HEADER-NO-COOKIE-DISCARD-NONCONFORMING / EXI-OPTION-VALUEPARTITIONCAPACITY-0 / EXI-PROFILE-BUILTIN-GRAMMARS-ZERO / EXI-SCHEMA-DEVIATION-HANDLING / BIG-ENDIAN-APPLICATION-LAYER / IPV6-NO-FRAGMENTATION / ICMPV6-MANDATORY-SET / TCP-NO-URGENT-POINTER: missing field - raw bytes. Only the tshark-decoded fields survive into the CSV; the `validation` column ('Successful' when healthy) is the single coarse proxy for the whole framing/EXI layer., SEQ-DC-CURRENTDEMAND-RECEIPTREQUIRED / FSM-CURRENTDEMANDRES-RECEIPT-NEXT / MSG-RECEIPTREQUIRED / SEQ-RECEIPTREQUIRED-LOOP / TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-METERINGRECEIPTREQ / TIMING-DC-EVCC-STOP-METERINGRECEIPT: missing field - ReceiptRequired (and MeterInfo). Not a schema column and not observed in the extra JSON, so the branch 'a CurrentDemandRes with ReceiptRequired=TRUE must be followed by MeteringReceiptReq' cannot be checked - which also means a MeteringReceipt-induced pause in the CurrentDemand cadence cannot be told apart from a stall., CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-SASCHEDULE / SASCHEDULETUPLEID-UNIQUE / SEQ-UNIQUE-SASCHEDULETUPLEID / SEQ-CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-LIMITS / RC-FAILED-CHARGINGPROFILEINVALID / RC-FAILED-TARIFFSELECTIONINVALID / PERF-CHARGINGPROFILE-SLOT-120S / SCHED-SASCHEDULETUPLEID-RANGE / SCHED-ENTRY-COUNT-LIMITS / MSG-SASCHEDULE-ENTRY-COUNTS: missing field - SAScheduleTuple / SAScheduleTupleID / PMaxScheduleEntry / ChargingProfile. None reach the CSV, so the 120 s schedule-slot grace window and every profile-versus-schedule cross-check are out of reach., ALL TLS / certificate / Plug-and-Charge timing rules (TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-CERTIFICATEINSTALLATIONREQ, TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-CERTIFICATEUPDATEREQ, TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-PAYMENTDETAILSREQ, TIMING-MSGPERF-SECC-CERTIFICATE*, RC-FAILED-NOCERTAVAILABLE-*, TLS-*, SEC-*, CERT-*, MSG-AUTHORIZATIONREQ-GENCHALLENGE): missing field - no TLS handshake, no certificates, and the messages themselves never occur (DIN 70121 is EIM/ExternalPayment only on this fleet). Their timers are defined but can never arm., ALL AC-only timing rules (TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-CHARGINGSTATUSREQ, TIMING-MSGPERF-SECC-CHARGINGSTATUSRES, SEQ-AC-*, AC-SECC-*, FSM-CHARGINGSTATUSRES-*, TIMING-AC-EVCC-STOP-CHARGINGSTATUS): not applicable - these captures are DC only; ChargingStatusReq/Res never appear. Listed so a detector does not reserve feature slots for timers that cannot fire., TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-START-ON-DLINK-READY / TIMING-SETUP-SECC-START-ON-DLINK-READY / STATE-EVCC-DLINK-TO-IPASSIGN / STATE-EVCC-IPASSIGN-TIMEOUT / IPV6-SLAAC-LINKLOCAL: partially implementable only - missing field is the D-LINK_READY.indication itself and the IPv6/ND traffic. The first SLAC_MATCH.CNF / tcp / sdp / v2g row is a LOWER-BOUND proxy for t0, so the 20 s and 18 s setup budgets can be breached-detected (a measured breach implies a real one) but never certified as met., SEQ-CHARGEPROGRESS-START-SEMANTICS / SEQ-CHARGEPROGRESS-STOP-SEMANTICS / SEQ-CHARGEPROGRESS-RENEGOTIATION-RULES / RENEG-* / ENUM-CHARGEPROGRESS-*: partially implementable - ChargeProgress is listed as a possible extra key but was NOT observed in the fleet telemetry; DIN's ReadyToChargeState is present instead (154 occurrences in 114 sessions). Start/Stop discrimination must be driven off extra.ReadyToChargeState, and any rule keyed on the literal 'Renegotiate' cannot fire because DIN DC sessions on this fleet never renegotiate.

## lens: protocol

### proposed features (26)

- **seq_illegal_successor_count** (high) — Number of EVCC requests that are not in the ISO/DIN allowed-next-request set for the state established by the last response in the same TCP stream. This is the core message-sequence-state-machine feature.
  - compute: Maintain per key K=(capture_file, tcp_stream) a state record; reset it whenever msg=='supportedAppProtocolReq'. Track last_res (msg name), last_res_proc (evse_processing), last_pd_progress (extra.ChargeProgress, else ReadyToChargeState true->Start / false->Stop). ALLOWED table, evaluated on each kind=='v2g' row whose msg ends in 'Req': last_res==None -> {supportedAppProtocolReq}; supportedAppProtocolRes -> {SessionSetupReq}; SessionSetupRes -> {ServiceDiscoveryReq}; ServiceDiscoveryRes -> {ServicePaymentSelectionReq}; ServicePaymentSelectionRes -> {ContractAuthenticationReq}; ContractAuthenticationRes & proc=='Ongoing' -> {ContractAuthenticationReq}; ContractAuthenticationRes & proc=='Finished' -> {ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq}; ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes & Ongoing -> {ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq}; ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes & Finished -> {CableCheckReq, PowerDeliveryReq}; CableCheckRes & Ongoing -> {CableCheckReq}; CableCheckRes & Finished -> {PreChargeReq}; PreChargeRes -> {PreChargeReq, PowerDeliveryReq}; PowerDeliveryRes & last_pd_progress=='Start' -> {CurrentDemandReq, PowerDeliveryReq}; PowerDeliveryRes & 'Stop' -> {ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq, WeldingDetectionReq, SessionStopReq}; PowerDeliveryRes & 'Renegotiate' -> {ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq}; CurrentDemandRes -> {CurrentDemandReq, PowerDeliveryReq}; WeldingDetectionRes -> {WeldingDetectionReq, SessionStopReq}; SessionStopRes -> {} (terminal). Increment the counter when msg not in ALLOWED[state]. Do NOT count a repeat of the immediately preceding request when the last response is missing (that is a retry after a lost/dropped packet, see unanswered_request_count). Emitted incrementally; the running count is the feature at any instant.
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: A dropped response packet (kind=='tcp' msg=='LOST' on the same stream, or a ring-buffer file boundary) makes the next legal request look illegal because last_res is stale. Suppress the increment if a tcp LOST/RETX row for the same tcp_stream occurred between last_res and this Req, or if the inter-message gap exceeds the capture-hole threshold. Also suppress when the ProtocolNamespace is not DIN 70121 (the successor table differs).
- **seq_first_violation_phase_idx** (high) — Ordinal index of the protocol phase in which the FIRST sequencing anomaly (illegal successor, pairing mismatch, or FAILED_SequenceError) occurred. Where a session breaks is far more diagnostic than how often.
  - compute: Maintain phase_idx per stream, monotonically advanced on the first occurrence of each anchor: 0=pre-handshake, 1=supportedAppProtocol, 2=SessionSetup, 3=ServiceDiscovery, 4=ServicePaymentSelection, 5=ContractAuthentication, 6=ChargeParameterDiscovery, 7=CableCheck, 8=PreCharge, 9=PowerDelivery(Start), 10=CurrentDemand loop, 11=PowerDelivery(Stop), 12=WeldingDetection, 13=SessionStop. On the first sequencing anomaly, latch first_violation_phase_idx=phase_idx; otherwise leave at -1.
  - clean looks like: -1
  - FP risk: Inherits the FP sources of its inputs; carries none of its own.
- **res_req_pairing_mismatch_count** (high) — Count of responses whose message name does not pair with the outstanding request ([V2G2-482]: the EVCC must stop the session on a mismatched response). A direct request/response pairing invariant, checkable from message names alone.
  - compute: Per stream keep pending_req=(msg,t) set when a '*Req' row is seen and cleared when the matching '*Res' arrives. On a '*Res' row: if pending_req is None -> increment orphan_res_count; else if res_base_name != req_base_name (strip the Req/Res suffix) -> increment pairing_mismatch_count and clear pending_req. Feature = pairing_mismatch_count (report orphan_res_count separately as a capture-quality signal, not a fault).
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: Two interleaved V2G sessions merged into one tcp_stream index across capture files would fabricate mismatches - key strictly on (capture_file, tcp_stream) and reset on each supportedAppProtocolReq. A lost request packet turns a legitimate response into an orphan, hence the separate counter.
- **unanswered_request_count_past_timeout** (high) — Number of requests that were still unanswered when their per-message-type EVCC timeout (Table 109) had elapsed. This is the standard's own definition of a communication error and the strongest 'why did this session die' anchor.
  - compute: TIMEOUT_S = {CurrentDemandReq:0.25, PowerDeliveryReq:5.0, supportedAppProtocolReq:2.0, SessionSetupReq:2.0, ServiceDiscoveryReq:2.0, ServicePaymentSelectionReq:2.0, ContractAuthenticationReq:2.0, ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq:2.0, CableCheckReq:2.0, PreChargeReq:2.0, WeldingDetectionReq:2.0, SessionStopReq:2.0}. On every event (any kind) with timestamp t, if pending_req is set and (t - pending_req.t) >= TIMEOUT_S[pending_req.msg], increment the counter once for that pending_req (mark it as already-counted) and latch timeout_msg_type/timeout_phase. Also fire the check from a wall-clock tick so a session that simply goes silent is detected without lookahead. Report a second, coarser counter using 10x the spec timeout (gross_stall_count) because the 250 ms CurrentDemand bound is far tighter than field jitter.
  - clean looks like: 0 for the gross counter; small non-zero for the strict counter is common on CurrentDemand under device-clock jitter
  - FP risk: Device clocks are unsynced but timestamps within one capture are self-consistent, so per-pair latency is sound; the real risks are (a) a dropped Res packet (correlate with tcp LOST/RETX on the same stream) and (b) a capture-file boundary, where a request is the last row before an hours-long hole - exclude any pending request whose timeout would fire after the last event of the file.
- **worst_msg_latency_ratio** (medium) — Maximum over the session of (measured Req->Res latency) / (EVCC timeout for that request type). Values above 1.0 are hard timeouts; 0.6-1.0 is the degraded-but-legal band.
  - compute: On each matched '*Res', lat = t_res - t_req; ratio = lat / TIMEOUT_S[req_msg]; keep a running max, and separately a running max restricted to setup-phase messages and to loop messages. Update incrementally, no buffering.
  - clean looks like: < 0.5 for setup messages; CurrentDemand ratio typically < 1 but noisy
  - FP risk: None structurally; interpret CurrentDemand ratios with the transport-loss feature, since a retransmitted segment inflates latency without any SECC fault.
- **currentdemand_latency_ewma_and_trend** (high) — Exponentially weighted mean of CurrentDemandReq->Res latency in units of the 25 ms SECC performance time, plus its rising trend. Per the standard this is the single most sensitive leading indicator of an SECC control-loop degrading before it ever emits a FAILED code.
  - compute: On each CurrentDemandRes matched to its CurrentDemandReq: lat=t_res-t_req; x=lat/0.025; ewma_fast = 0.2*x + 0.8*ewma_fast; ewma_slow = 0.02*x + 0.98*ewma_slow (both initialised on the first sample). Emit two scalars: level=ewma_fast and trend=ewma_fast/max(ewma_slow,1e-6). Also keep a running count of pairs with lat>0.25 s (hard EVCC timeout breaches) and the longest run of consecutive such pairs.
  - clean looks like: level around 1-10 (25-250 ms), trend near 1.0, zero hard breaches
  - FP risk: A rising trend can be caused by PLC-layer congestion from the neighbouring connector rather than the SECC - always read alongside tcp_retx_lost_rate_in_loop. Do not read a latency rise as a fault when EVSECurrentLimitAchieved/PowerLimitAchieved is set (load sharing) unless latency also breaches 250 ms.
- **resp_code_severity_max** (high) — Maximum ResponseCode severity seen in the session, using the standard's prefix semantics (anything starting with OK is positive, anything starting with FAILED is negative) plus a specificity weighting.
  - compute: Severity map applied to resp_code on every v2g row: 0 for '' / 'OK' / any string starting with 'OK'; 1 for 'OK_CertificateExpiresSoon' (advisory); 5 for 'FAILED_SequenceError', 'FAILED_UnknownSession'; 5 for 'Failed_NoNegotiation' (note the AppProtocol schema spells it with a single capital F and NO underscore - a startswith('FAILED_') test misses it); 4 for 'FAILED_PowerDeliveryNotApplied', 'FAILED_ContactorError', 'FAILED_WrongChargeParameter', 'FAILED_WrongEnergyTransferType'/'FAILED_WrongEnergyTransferMode'; 3 for bare 'FAILED' and any other FAILED_*. Keep the running max, and latch (code, msg, phase_idx) of the first non-zero.
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: Case and prefix handling is the whole risk: normalise with casefold and test startswith('failed'), otherwise Failed_NoNegotiation is silently scored clean.
- **requests_after_failed_count** (high) — Number of application requests the EVCC sent on the same session after receiving a FAILED-prefixed response. The standard requires the EVCC to stop the session and the SECC to tear down the connection, so any continuation is itself a conformance defect and marks a stack that ignores error handling.
  - compute: Latch failed_seen=True and t_failed when a v2g Res row has resp_code casefold-starting with 'failed'. Thereafter increment for each subsequent '*Req' row on the same stream, EXCLUDING SessionStopReq and WeldingDetectionReq (many stacks close down gracefully after an error and that should not be penalised). Reset only on a new supportedAppProtocolReq (new session).
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: Excluding the shutdown tail is essential; without that exclusion nearly every error session scores non-zero. Do not count messages carrying a different session id (a fresh retry session on the same stream).
- **ongoing_run_duration_auth_cpd_s** (high) — Duration of the longest EVSEProcessing=Ongoing polling run for ContractAuthentication and for ChargeParameterDiscovery, measured from the FIRST Ongoing response of the run (not the most recent poll). Bounds: SECC must FAILED at 55 s, EVCC must abort at 60 s.
  - compute: Per stream keep ongoing_t0[msg_base] . On a Res row with evse_processing=='Ongoing': if ongoing_t0[base] is None set it to t. On evse_processing=='Finished' or on any Res of a different base: emit dur = t - ongoing_t0[base], update max_ongoing[base], clear ongoing_t0[base]. Also expose the live value (now - ongoing_t0) on every event so a still-running stall is visible before the session ends. Emit two scalars: max_ongoing_auth_s and max_ongoing_cpd_s, plus booleans reached_finished_auth / reached_finished_cpd.
  - clean looks like: CPD: under a few seconds. Auth: highly variable - a long Ongoing run on ContractAuthentication is usually a driver who has not yet presented an RFID card (DIN has no Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction literal to say so), and is NOT an equipment fault.
  - FP risk: HIGH for the authorization arm specifically: in EIM/ExternalPayment DIN sessions the SECC signals 'waiting for the customer' with plain Ongoing, so a 60 s auth stall is routinely human. Keep authorization in features only; do not promote it to a hard rule. The CPD arm has no human in the loop and is much safer.
- **cablecheck_phase_duration_and_outcome** (high) — Elapsed time from the FIRST CableCheckReq of the session to the CableCheckRes carrying ResponseCode OK and EVSEProcessing=Finished, plus whether Finished was ever reached and what the isolation status was at that moment. Anchored at the first request, per [V2G2-700] - a detector that resets on every poll misses the fault entirely.
  - compute: On the first CableCheckReq of a stream, latch t_cc0=t. On each CableCheckRes: if evse_processing=='Finished' and resp_code starts with 'OK', latch cc_duration=t-t_cc0, cc_finished=True, cc_isolation_at_finish=isolation, and stop the timer. On every subsequent event while cc_finished is False and t_cc0 is set, expose live_cc_elapsed = t - t_cc0 so the stall is visible in-flight. Also count cablecheck poll pairs.
  - clean looks like: Typically a few seconds up to ~20 s; cc_finished True; isolation Valid (or the element absent) at Finished
  - FP risk: Do not read the isolation field out of a CableCheckRes whose resp_code starts with FAILED - those fields are permitted arbitrary filler ([V2G2-736]). isolation=='Invalid' during the loop is the normal pre-measurement state and must not be scored; only 'Fault' is hard, and 'Warning' is a trend signal.
- **precharge_phase_duration_and_exit** (high) — Elapsed time from the FIRST PreChargeReq to the first PowerDeliveryReq, plus how the phase exited (PowerDelivery / FAILED / abandoned) and whether EVSEPresentVoltage was converging on EVTargetVoltage. EVCC bound is 7 s, SECC performance bound 5 s.
  - compute: Latch t_pc0 on the first PreChargeReq of the stream. On each PreChargeRes, update conv = |evse_v - last_precharge_req.ev_target_v| and keep an EWMA of conv and of its first difference (a convergence slope) - both are single-pass. On the first PowerDeliveryReq after t_pc0, emit pc_duration=t-t_pc0 and pc_exit='powerdelivery'. If instead a FAILED PreChargeRes or session death occurs first, set pc_exit accordingly. Expose live elapsed while the loop runs.
  - clean looks like: under ~5 s, exit 'powerdelivery', conv shrinking toward zero
