  - FP risk: The EV's completion threshold is internal and unspecified, so a converged voltage does not prove the loop should have ended - judge by the 7 s timer, not by the voltage alone. Skip voltage comparison entirely on rows whose resp_code starts with FAILED.
- **commsetup_to_sessionsetupres_s** (medium) — Elapsed time from the first observable communication-setup activity on this connector to the SessionSetupRes. EVCC budget 20 s, SECC 18 s.
  - compute: Latch t_setup0 = timestamp of the first kind=='sdp' row (or, if none, the first kind=='tcp'/'v2g' row) that is not separated from the following V2G traffic by a capture hole. On SessionSetupRes, emit t - t_setup0. Also count sdp rows before the first supportedAppProtocolReq (sdp_request_burst_len) - the standard stops SDP at 50 unanswered requests / 20 s.
  - clean looks like: well under 18 s; a small SDP burst
  - FP risk: t0 is a lower bound only: the true anchor is D-LINK ready, which is not captured, and the capture may start mid-setup. Treat a marginal 19-21 s value as unknown rather than a violation. SLAC/HPAV rows are NOT a safe t0 because the powerline is shared and the frames may belong to the neighbouring connector.
- **session_id_anomaly_flags** (medium) — SessionID discipline: non-zero SessionID in the first SessionSetupReq, SessionID changing after SessionSetupRes, SessionSetupRes returning zero, and any FAILED_UnknownSession. A mid-session SessionID change is the cheapest detector of an SECC watchdog reboot that leaves the TCP connection up.
  - compute: On SessionSetupReq (first of the stream): flag_nonzero_first = (session not in {'', '0', '00', '0000000000000000'}). On SessionSetupRes: latch sid = session; flag_res_zero = sid is all-zero. On every later v2g row on the stream: if session != sid, increment sid_change_count and update sid. Separately latch unknown_session_flag = any resp_code=='FAILED_UnknownSession'. Emit the four values as a small bit-mask plus sid_change_count.
  - clean looks like: all flags 0, sid_change_count 0
  - FP risk: Compare with case- and zero-padding-normalised hex. A legitimate retry session on the same tcp_stream begins with a fresh supportedAppProtocolReq/SessionSetupReq - reset sid there rather than counting a change.
- **termination_conformance_class** (high) — How the session ended, as an ordinal against the standard's normative shutdown chain PowerDeliveryReq(Stop) -> PowerDeliveryRes(OK) -> [WeldingDetection]* -> SessionStopReq -> SessionStopRes(OK).
  - compute: Advance a termination automaton on each event: 0 = died before PowerDeliveryReq(Stop); 1 = PD(Stop) sent, no OK PD Res; 2 = PD(Stop) answered OK, nothing after; 3 = reached WeldingDetection but no SessionStopReq; 4 = SessionStopReq sent, no SessionStopRes; 5 = SessionStopRes with FAILED; 6 = SessionStopRes with OK (clean). Finalise the class when the session is closed out - by a tcp RST on the stream, by a new supportedAppProtocolReq on the same stream, or by an idle watchdog (no rows on the stream for > IDLE_S, e.g. 120 s). No lookahead is needed: the class only ever increases, so the current value is always emittable.
  - clean looks like: 6
  - FP risk: Class 0-2 at a capture-file boundary is very often truncation, not an abort - always pair with abort_anchor_mask. Note DIN SessionStopReq carries no ChargingSession element, so its absence must never be scored.
- **abort_anchor_mask** (high) — Which corroborating evidence exists for an abnormal end. This is the feature that separates a genuine abort from a ring-buffer truncation, and it is the single most important false-positive control in the whole design.
  - compute: Bit-mask latched over the session, evaluated online: bit0 = a kind=='tcp' msg=='RST' row on this stream; bit1 = any FAILED-prefixed resp_code; bit2 = an unanswered request whose per-type timeout expired while the capture was still producing rows on other streams (proves the capture did not simply stop); bit3 = evse_status in {EVSE_Malfunction, EVSE_EmergencyShutdown} on a non-FAILED row; bit4 = isolation=='Fault' on a non-FAILED row; bit5 = ev_err not in {NO_ERROR, NoData, ''} on a non-FAILED row; bit6 = ev_ready transitioned TRUE->FALSE inside the CurrentDemand loop; bit7 = last event of the session is also the last event of the capture file AND a long hole follows (i.e. truncation, an ANTI-anchor). Emit the mask and popcount(mask & 0x7F).
  - clean looks like: 0 for a clean session; bit7 alone for a truncated-but-healthy one
  - FP risk: bit2 must be gated on other-stream liveness, otherwise every truncated capture scores it. bit3/bit4/bit5 must ignore rows whose resp_code starts with FAILED (arbitrary filler per [V2G2-736]).
- **loop_entry_integrity_flags** (high) — Whether the DC charging loop was entered through the legal gate. Entry is defined by a PowerDeliveryRes with ResponseCode OK answering a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=Start; exit by the same pair with Stop.
  - compute: Track seen_cc_finished, seen_precharge, pd_start_ok (PowerDeliveryRes with OK-prefixed resp_code whose matching Req had ChargeProgress/ReadyToChargeState = Start), and cd_count. Flags: f_cd_before_pd_start = (first CurrentDemandReq occurs while pd_start_ok is False); f_pd_start_without_cablecheck_finished = (PowerDeliveryReq(Start) sent while seen_cc_finished is False); f_pd_start_without_precharge = (PowerDeliveryReq(Start) sent with zero preceding PreChargeReq); f_loop_never_started = (pd_start_ok True and cd_count==0); f_current_before_start = (evse_i > 1 A observed on a row before pd_start_ok, ignoring FAILED rows).
  - clean looks like: all flags 0
  - FP risk: f_pd_start_without_cablecheck_finished can misfire when the CableCheckRes(Finished) packet was lost - gate on the absence of tcp LOST for that stream. f_current_before_start uses a 1 A floor so that leakage/measurement noise does not trip it.
- **renegotiation_violation_count** (medium) — Renegotiation ordering violations: a ChargeProgress=Renegotiate with no prior Start, a Renegotiate not followed by ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq, or a mid-loop ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq with no preceding PowerDeliveryReq(Renegotiate).
  - compute: Track seen_pd_start and last_pd_progress. On a PowerDeliveryReq with progress=='Renegotiate': if not seen_pd_start -> increment (reneg_without_start). Set awaiting_cpd=True on the following PowerDeliveryRes; if the next Req is not ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq -> increment (reneg_not_followed_by_cpd). On a ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq while cd_count>0: if last_pd_progress != 'Renegotiate' and the last response was not PowerDeliveryRes(Stop) -> increment (cpd_mid_loop_unannounced). Also count the legal case separately as reneg_wellformed_count so a genuine renegotiation is not mistaken for a restart.
  - clean looks like: 0 violations; also 0 well-formed renegotiations in almost all DIN DC captures
  - FP risk: The main value here is negative: a mid-loop ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq that IS preceded by a Renegotiate is legitimate and must not be scored as a session restart, and the CurrentDemand gap it creates must not be scored as a stall. DIN chargers essentially never renegotiate, so any occurrence at all is worth surfacing.
- **session_restart_count_within_plugin** (medium) — Number of complete communication bring-ups (supportedAppProtocolReq on a new tcp_stream) within one plug-in window, plus whether the attempts died at the same phase each time.
  - compute: Group streams into a plug-in window: a new stream whose first event is within RESTART_S (e.g. 180 s) of the previous stream's last event, with no hours-long hole between, belongs to the same plug-in. Increment restart_count; record each attempt's seq_first_violation_phase_idx / termination class. Emit restart_count and restart_same_phase = 1 if the last two or more attempts died at the same phase index.
  - clean looks like: restart_count 1 (a single attempt)
  - FP risk: Do not join streams across an idle hole (idle = zero packets is normal). A single restart after a deliberate stop is not a retry. restart_same_phase is the high-signal half: repeated failure at the same message is a firm interoperability fault, whereas restarts at varying points suggest a flaky PLC link.
- **handshake_conformance_flags** (high) — Application-protocol handshake conformance: the first V2G message on the stream, a Failed_NoNegotiation outcome, and whether a session was initialised despite a failed negotiation.
  - compute: f_first_msg_not_sap = (the first kind=='v2g' row on the stream is not supportedAppProtocolReq) - report but treat as low-confidence, since the capture may begin mid-stream. f_nonegotiation = any supportedAppProtocolRes with resp_code casefold=='failed_nonegotiation'. f_session_after_nonegotiation = a SessionSetupReq appears on the same stream after f_nonegotiation latched. f_sap_repeat_midsession = a second supportedAppProtocolReq on the same stream without an intervening session end. Also record extra.ProtocolNamespace and extra.SchemaID from the handshake as categorical context - every downstream decode depends on the negotiated dialect.
  - clean looks like: all flags 0; ProtocolNamespace = urn:din:70121:2012:MsgDef
  - FP risk: f_first_msg_not_sap fires on any capture whose ring buffer opened mid-session - keep it out of hard rules. f_nonegotiation itself is a genuine and unambiguous interoperability fault (DIN-only EV against ISO-only charger or vice versa).
- **dialect_foreign_message_count** (medium) — Count of message names that do not belong to the negotiated dialect - e.g. ServiceDetailReq/Res, PaymentDetailsReq/Res, CertificateInstallation*/CertificateUpdate*, ChargingStatusReq/Res or MeteringReceiptReq/Res appearing in a session that negotiated the DIN 70121 namespace.
  - compute: After the handshake, if extra.ProtocolNamespace contains 'din:70121', increment for every v2g msg not in the DIN DC set {supportedAppProtocol*, SessionSetup*, ServiceDiscovery*, ServicePaymentSelection*, ContractAuthentication*, ChargeParameterDiscovery*, CableCheck*, PreCharge*, PowerDelivery*, CurrentDemand*, WeldingDetection*, SessionStop*}. Also increment for ISO-only ResponseCode literals (FAILED_SignatureError, FAILED_ContactorError, FAILED_CertificateRevoked, OK_CertificateExpiresSoon) and for evse_processing=='Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction' or notification=='ReNegotiation' on a DIN link.
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: This measures the decoder as much as the device: a foreign message name most often means the wrong schema was applied to the EXI stream, which then corrupts every other field in the row. Use it as a gate that suppresses other findings, not only as a fault.
- **post_terminal_message_count** (medium) — Application messages seen on the same session after SessionStopRes. SessionStopRes is terminal and must be followed by transport teardown, so anything after it is a state-machine defect on one side.
  - compute: Latch stopped=True and stop_sid=session on a SessionStopRes row. Thereafter, increment for each kind=='v2g' row on the same stream carrying the same session id, unless the row is a supportedAppProtocolReq/SessionSetupReq that begins a new session (which resets the state record).
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: Must reset on a genuine new session, otherwise back-to-back sessions on a reused stream index all score. Note the transport rule cannot be fully checked in the other direction: the schema exposes RST but not FIN, so 'connection left open after SessionStopRes' is not observable.
- **evse_processing_anomaly_count** (low) — EVSEProcessing misuse: a regression from Finished back to Ongoing within the same phase, an EVSEProcessing value outside the DIN enumeration, or the element appearing on a message other than ContractAuthenticationRes / ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes / CableCheckRes.
  - compute: Per (stream, message base) keep proc_finished_seen. On each Res row with a non-empty evse_processing: if evse_processing not in {'Ongoing','Finished'} -> increment enum_violation; if base not in the three legal carriers -> increment placement_violation; if evse_processing=='Ongoing' and proc_finished_seen[base] -> increment regression_count. Feature = sum of the three, with the components also exposed.
  - clean looks like: 0
  - FP risk: A legitimate renegotiation re-runs ChargeParameterDiscovery and will show Ongoing after a previous Finished - reset proc_finished_seen['ChargeParameterDiscovery'] whenever a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=Renegotiate is seen, otherwise every renegotiation scores a regression.
- **decode_validation_failure_rate** (medium) — Fraction of v2g rows whose Wireshark EXI/schema validation field is not 'Successful', plus any header Notification FaultCode. Primarily a gate: a decode problem invalidates every other field-based finding downstream.
  - compute: Running counters n_v2g and n_bad where bad = (validation not in {'Successful',''}). rate = n_bad/max(n_v2g,1). Separately latch notification_faultcode = extra.FaultCode when present (ParsingError / NoTLSRootCertificatAvailable / UnknownError) and notification_faultmsg. Emit the rate, the latched fault code as a small categorical, and a boolean any_header_notification.
  - clean looks like: rate 0, no header Notification
  - FP risk: The extraction toolchain has a known tshark FT_NONE quirk that can mark structurally fine messages as not validated, so a nonzero rate is not automatically a device fault. Use the rate to DOWN-weight other features rather than to raise an alarm on its own; the header Notification with FaultCode is the trustworthy half.
- **stopcharging_to_stop_latency_s** (medium) — Seconds from the first response carrying EVSENotification=StopCharging to the EVCC's PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=Stop. The standard bounds this by NotificationMaxDelay, which is not extracted, so this is a heuristic-bounded feature and never a hard rule.
  - compute: Latch t_stopnotif on the first row with notification=='StopCharging' whose resp_code does not start with FAILED. On the first subsequent PowerDeliveryReq with progress=='Stop', emit latency = t - t_stopnotif; if the session dies first, emit the elapsed time and set flag_never_stopped. Also count how many CurrentDemand pairs elapsed after the notification.
  - clean looks like: a few seconds; a user-initiated or operator-initiated stop that is honoured promptly is entirely normal and must not be scored as a fault
  - FP risk: NotificationMaxDelay is missing from the schema, so the true deadline is unknown - a 30 s latency may be perfectly conformant if the charger granted 60 s. Treat only very long latencies (say > 60 s, above any plausible NotificationMaxDelay for an unsignedShort in practice) as suspicious, and never label StopCharging itself as a fault: it is the normal EVSE-side stop signal in these captures and routinely accompanies load sharing together with EVSECurrentLimitAchieved.
- **sequence_gap_max_ratio** (medium) — Largest gap from a response to the EVCC's next request, expressed against the 40 s EVCC sequence performance time and the 60 s SECC sequence timeout. Distinguishes an EV that stalled from a charger that stopped answering.
  - compute: On each '*Res', set t_last_res. On the next '*Req', gap = t - t_last_res; running max_gap. Emit ratio40 = max_gap/40 and ratio60 = max_gap/60, and latch the phase in which the worst gap occurred. A gap in [40,60) s is an EVCC performance breach with the session still alive; a gap >= 60 s should be followed by an SECC-initiated teardown - if the session instead continues past 60 s of EV silence, latch flag_secc_seq_timer_not_enforced.
  - clean looks like: ratio40 well under 1
  - FP risk: A capture-file boundary inside the gap makes it meaningless - discard any gap that spans a known hole. Idle is zero packets, so an hours-long gap after a session has effectively ended is not a sequence-timer breach.
- **tcp_transport_degradation_rate** (medium) — Rate of tcp RETX/LOST rows on the session's stream, split by phase (setup vs CurrentDemand loop). Not a protocol fault in itself, but the required context for attributing latency and apparent sequence breaks.
  - compute: Running counts of kind=='tcp' rows with msg in {RETX, LOST} on the stream, divided by elapsed session seconds and by v2g message count; maintain one pair of counters for the pre-loop phase and one for the loop phase (switch on pd_start_ok).
  - clean looks like: near zero
  - FP risk: None as a feature; its purpose is to suppress false attribution - a lost segment can manufacture an apparent unanswered request, an apparent illegal successor, and an apparent latency spike all at once.

### proposed hard rules (13)

- `failed_sequence_error_observed` → SEQUENCE_VIOLATION (confidence 0.97)
  - condition: any kind=='v2g' row with resp_code.casefold()=='failed_sequenceerror'
  - why: FAILED_SequenceError is emitted by the SECC only when it receives a request it does not expect in its current wait state. It exists in the DIN 70121 responseCodeType and never appears in a healthy session. The immediately preceding request names the illegal transition, so the alarm is also self-diagnosing.
  - FP risk: Essentially none. The only way a healthy session carries this code is a charger firmware defect that emits it spuriously - which is itself a fault worth alarming on. Requires only correct casefolded string matching.
- `unknown_session_or_sessionid_changed` → PROTOCOL_STATE_LOSS (confidence 0.93)
  - condition: any resp_code.casefold()=='failed_unknownsession'  OR  (sid latched from SessionSetupRes AND a later v2g row on the same tcp_stream has a non-empty session != sid AND that row is not part of a new session started by a fresh supportedAppProtocolReq)
  - why: The SessionID returned in SessionSetupRes must not change while the session is not terminated, and every message of both parties must carry it. A mid-session change or a FAILED_UnknownSession means one side lost its session state - typically an SECC watchdog reboot that left the TCP connection standing, or a vehicle reusing a stale SessionID. Everything measured after that point belongs to a different logical session.
  - FP risk: Requires normalised hex comparison (case, zero padding) and a correct reset on back-to-back sessions sharing a tcp_stream index; without those a benign retry session scores. Interleaved sessions merged into one stream index across capture files would also fabricate it, so key strictly on (capture_file, tcp_stream).
- `power_path_phase_skipped` → SEQUENCE_VIOLATION (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: (first PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress/ReadyToChargeState == Start occurs while no CableCheckRes with resp_code startswith 'OK' and evse_processing=='Finished' has been seen on this session) OR (first PreChargeReq occurs while the last CableCheckRes evse_processing is 'Ongoing' or no CableCheckRes has been seen) OR (first CurrentDemandReq occurs while no PowerDeliveryRes with an OK-prefixed resp_code answering a Start request has been seen) - in every case with no tcp LOST/RETX row on this stream since the last observed response and no capture-file boundary in between
  - why: The DC sequence gates energy on safety phases: ChargeParameterDiscovery(Finished) -> CableCheck(OK,Finished) -> PreCharge -> PowerDelivery(Start) -> CurrentDemand. Skipping the isolation test or entering the current loop without an acknowledged Start is a safety-relevant state-machine break, not a timing nuisance, and a conformant SECC must answer FAILED_SequenceError.
  - FP risk: The dominant risk is a dropped response packet making a legal request look like a skip - hence the explicit loss/hole guard in the condition. A second risk is the legitimate pause path where the EV goes from ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes(Finished) straight to PowerDeliveryReq(Stop); that carries Stop, not Start, so the condition already excludes it.
- `session_continued_after_failed_response` → ERROR_HANDLING_VIOLATION (confidence 0.88)
  - condition: count of '*Req' rows on the same session after the first Res with resp_code.casefold().startswith('failed'), excluding SessionStopReq and WeldingDetectionReq, is >= 2
  - why: On any FAILED-prefixed ResponseCode the EVCC must stop the session and terminate the connection, and the SECC must terminate too. A stack that keeps issuing ordinary requests after a declared failure is operating in an undefined state - it may keep energy flowing or withhold it with no agreed state between the parties.
  - FP risk: The graceful shutdown tail (SessionStop, WeldingDetection) is excluded, and the threshold is 2 rather than 1 so that a single in-flight request already on the wire when the FAILED arrived does not trip it. Remaining risk is a decoder that mis-reads an OK as FAILED - gate on decode_validation_failure_rate being 0.
- `app_protocol_negotiation_failed` → HANDSHAKE_FAILURE (confidence 0.95)
  - condition: any supportedAppProtocolRes with resp_code.casefold() in {'failed_nonegotiation','failed'}  (note the AppProtocol schema spells the literal 'Failed_NoNegotiation' - match casefolded, do not test startswith('FAILED_'))
  - why: None of the application protocols offered by the vehicle is supported by the charger. This is the earliest possible and least ambiguous interoperability fault: no SessionSetup can follow and no energy can ever be delivered. It is a firm site/vehicle compatibility finding rather than a transient.
  - FP risk: Only string-matching risk. A separate, additional violation - a SessionSetupReq appearing on the same stream after this code - is worth reporting alongside but does not change the alarm.
- `cablecheck_never_finished_past_budget` → CABLECHECK_TIMEOUT (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: t_first_CableCheckReq is set AND no CableCheckRes with OK-prefixed resp_code and evse_processing=='Finished' has been seen AND (t_now - t_first_CableCheckReq) >= 45.0 s AND the capture is still producing rows (so this is not a truncation)
  - why: The EVCC cable-check budget is 40 s from the FIRST CableCheckReq and the SECC must have failed the session at 38 s (or at 55 s of Ongoing). A cable check that has not produced OK+Finished by 45 s is a stalled or failing isolation test - the dominant hard failure mode on DC captures. There is no human in this loop, so a long stall has no benign explanation.
  - FP risk: A 5 s guard band is added over the 40 s spec value because DIN publishes its own table and some chargers run genuinely slow isolation tests. Must not fire on a capture-file boundary (the phase may have completed in the missing data), hence the liveness condition. Do not read the isolation field from any FAILED CableCheckRes when annotating this alarm - those values are permitted filler.
- `charge_parameter_discovery_ongoing_past_budget` → ONGOING_TIMEOUT (confidence 0.88)
  - condition: t_first_Ongoing['ChargeParameterDiscovery'] is set AND no ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes with evse_processing=='Finished' has been seen AND (t_now - t_first_Ongoing) >= 65.0 s AND the capture is still producing rows
  - why: The SECC must send FAILED and stop at 55 s of Ongoing and the EVCC must abort at 60 s. A ChargeParameterDiscovery that never produces a schedule is a charger-side scheduling stall with no human in the loop, so unlike the authorization arm it has no benign long-duration explanation.
  - FP risk: A 5 s guard band is used because DIN carries its own timing table. Deliberately excludes ContractAuthentication, where a long Ongoing run in an EIM/ExternalPayment session usually means the driver has not yet presented a card - DIN has no Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction literal to distinguish the two, so authorization stalls stay in features only.
- `power_delivery_start_refused_or_unanswered` → POWER_DELIVERY_REFUSED (confidence 0.92)
  - condition: a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress/ReadyToChargeState == Start is answered by a PowerDeliveryRes whose resp_code.casefold() startswith 'failed'  OR  such a request is never answered and the session ends (RST, or an idle watchdog fires) with zero CurrentDemandReq afterwards
  - why: This is the moment the charger is asked to make energy available. A FAILED here - FAILED_PowerDeliveryNotApplied being the specific DIN literal for 'the EVSE is not able to deliver energy' - or a PowerDeliveryReq(Start) that is simply never answered within its 5 s timeout is an unambiguous charger-side refusal at the contactor-close transition, and no charge follows.
  - FP risk: The unanswered branch must not fire at a capture-file boundary; require either an explicit RST or continued capture activity on other streams. The FAILED branch is unambiguous. Do not chart the voltage/current/status fields carried in that FAILED response - they are arbitrary filler.
- `tcp_reset_before_sessionstopres` → SESSION_ABORT (confidence 0.93)
  - condition: a kind=='tcp' row with msg=='RST' occurs on a tcp_stream that carried v2g traffic, and no SessionStopRes with an OK-prefixed resp_code has been seen for the active session on that stream
  - why: The standard states plainly that a TCP connection terminated before a SessionStopRes has been exchanged is always to be interpreted as an error by both parties. An explicit RST is a positive, in-band signal - unlike a capture ending, which proves nothing - so it converts the abort test from a guess into an observation.
  - FP risk: Anchoring on RST specifically is what makes this safe: a session ending at a capture-file boundary with an hours-long hole afterwards is normal and produces no RST. Note the schema exposes no FIN, so the graceful-close half of the rule is unobservable - a clean session that ends with FIN and no RST correctly does not trip this. DIN has no pause/resume, so the ISO Pause exemption does not apply here.
- `response_does_not_pair_with_outstanding_request` → SEQUENCE_VIOLATION (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: a '*Res' row arrives whose base message name differs from the base name of the outstanding '*Req' on the same (capture_file, tcp_stream), with no tcp LOST/RETX row on that stream since the outstanding request and no capture-file boundary in between
  - why: [V2G2-482] requires the EVCC to stop the session on any response that does not correspond to the last request sent. A mismatched pair means one side's state machine has diverged from the other's - the parties no longer agree on which exchange is in progress, which in the DC loop means voltage and current values are being read against the wrong request.
  - FP risk: Two interleaved sessions folded into one stream index, or a lost request packet, both fabricate mismatches - the stream keying and the loss guard in the condition are mandatory, not optional. Orphan responses (no outstanding request at all) are deliberately excluded from this rule and tracked as a capture-quality counter instead.
- `header_notification_faultcode_present` → DECODE_FAULT (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: extra.FaultCode is present and non-empty on any v2g row (values: ParsingError, NoTLSRootCertificatAvailable, UnknownError)
  - why: The optional Notification element in the V2G message header is absent during normal operation; it exists solely so a peer can announce that it could not decode what it received. Its presence is a direct, in-band admission of a parsing or decoding failure by one of the two stacks, and it is a channel most analysers never look at because it lives in the header rather than the body.
  - FP risk: The risk is extraction-side: confirm the field is populated only from a genuinely present header Notification and not defaulted by the dissector. FaultMsg (max 64 chars) usually carries a vendor string that confirms the finding. This is distinct from the tshark validation column, which has a known FT_NONE quirk and must not be alarmed on.
- `renegotiate_without_prior_start` → RENEGOTIATION_VIOLATION (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: a PowerDeliveryReq whose extra.ChargeProgress == 'Renegotiate' occurs on a session in which no PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=='Start' has previously been sent
  - why: [V2G2-841] forbids sending Renegotiate before a Start, and [V2G2-812] requires the SECC to answer FAILED in exactly this case. It is a pure ordering invariant over two fields already in the schema, with no timing or electrical judgement involved. If the charger nonetheless answers OK, the charger is non-conformant too - both halves are worth alarming.
  - FP risk: Rare in practice (DIN DC sessions essentially never renegotiate), so this rule will almost never fire; when it does, the session is genuinely mis-sequenced. Requires that ChargeProgress be read from extra with ReadyToChargeState as the fallback mapping, so that a DIN capture carrying only the boolean does not silently disable the check.
- `messages_after_sessionstopres` → PROTOCOL_STATE_LOSS (confidence 0.87)
  - condition: >= 2 kind=='v2g' rows carrying the same session id on the same tcp_stream after a SessionStopRes, where none of those rows is a supportedAppProtocolReq or SessionSetupReq beginning a new session
  - why: SessionStopRes is the terminal state of the V2G communication session and both parties must then tear the connection down. Continued application traffic on the stopped session means one side never left the session, which leaks charger session slots and leaves the next vehicle unable to connect.
  - FP risk: The state record must reset on a new supportedAppProtocolReq, otherwise back-to-back sessions on a reused stream index all score. Threshold of 2 avoids tripping on a single in-flight message crossing the SessionStopRes on the wire.

### ground-truth changes the standard implies (14)

- Split the single FAILED_* bucket into fault families keyed on the specific ResponseCode literal, and add a dedicated SEQUENCE_VIOLATION class for FAILED_SequenceError and for detected illegal successors that the charger failed to reject.
  - today: Any FAILED_* ResponseCode produces one undifferentiated fault label.
  - under ISO: Each FAILED_* literal names a distinct cause and a distinct responsible party: FAILED_SequenceError blames the requester's state machine, FAILED_PowerDeliveryNotApplied blames the charger's power stage, FAILED_WrongChargeParameter blames parameter negotiation, FAILED_UnknownSession blames lost session state. The generic bare FAILED means only 'no specific code was defined'.
  - risk: Low. It refines an existing label rather than changing what is flagged, so recall is unchanged. The only cost is that the training set's per-class counts become sparser; keep the coarse FAILED_ANY label as a parent class so the model can fall back.
- Match ResponseCodes on the casefolded prefix 'ok' / 'failed' rather than on exact literals or on startswith('FAILED_'), so that the supportedAppProtocol handshake code Failed_NoNegotiation is caught, and so OK_NewSessionEstablished / OK_OldSessionJoined / OK_CertificateExpiresSoon are never scored as faults.
  - today: A FAILED_* match keyed on the underscore form. Failed_NoNegotiation (single capital F, no underscore after 'Failed') is silently scored clean, so DIN-vs-ISO dialect mismatches are labelled healthy.
  - under ISO: The standard defines the semantics by prefix: anything starting with OK is positive, anything starting with FAILED is negative. The AppProtocol schema uses a different capitalisation for its three codes, and Failed_NoNegotiation is an unambiguous interoperability failure that prevents any session from starting.
  - risk: Very low, and it fixes a pure false negative. The one thing to verify is that no vendor emits a positive code whose name begins with 'failed' - none exists in either enumeration.
- Suppress all field-derived labels (isolation Fault, EVSE_Malfunction/EmergencyShutdown, EVErrorCode, voltage/current anomalies) when they are read from a row whose ResponseCode starts with FAILED, and instead attribute that row solely to the ResponseCode.
  - today: isolation, evse_status and ev_err are evaluated on every row, including FAILED responses, so one failing exchange produces several stacked labels.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-736] permits the SECC to fill all mandatory parameters of a FAILED response with arbitrary XSD-conformant values chosen to minimise message size, and [V2G2-735] requires the EVCC to ignore them. Those fields are meaningless filler by design.
  - risk: Low, but it is a recall trade: a genuine isolation Fault reported in the same message as a FAILED code would no longer be labelled from that row. Mitigate by still honouring the field on any non-FAILED row in the session, which is where a real isolation fault is reported anyway (CableCheckRes with OK + Ongoing).
- Require a corroborating anchor before labelling SESSION_ABORT for a missing SessionStopRes: a tcp RST, a FAILED-prefixed ResponseCode, a request unanswered past its Table 109 timeout while the capture was still live, or an EV/EVSE error field. With no anchor, emit a lower-severity UNEXPLAINED_DISCONNECT instead.
  - today: Absence of SessionStopRes alone is labelled SESSION_ABORT.
  - under ISO: The standard agrees that a transport close before SessionStopRes is always an error - but only when a close actually happened. It also recognises legitimate abrupt ends that are not equipment faults: a CP State E/F event (unplug) requires the EVCC to stop the session and tear down with no SessionStop exchange at all.
  - risk: Moderate, and the most important change here. It reduces false positives on ring-buffer truncation and on user unplugs, but it will also miss genuine aborts whose only evidence was outside the capture window. Recommend keeping both labels and letting the model use the anchor mask as a feature rather than dropping the unanchored cases entirely.
- Re-anchor the stuck-CableCheck label at the FIRST CableCheckReq of the session and lower the threshold from 60 s to 45 s (40 s spec plus a 5 s guard band for the DIN table), and require that the phase never reached ResponseCode OK together with EVSEProcessing=Finished.
  - today: 60 s of stuck CableCheck, with the anchor not clearly tied to the first request of the phase.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout is 40 s measured from the first CableCheckReq of the charging session, the SECC performance bound is 38 s, and the SECC should already have emitted FAILED at 55 s of Ongoing. Completion is signalled solely by CableCheckRes with OK and EVSEProcessing=Finished - not by the loop merely stopping.
  - risk: Moderate. Lowering to 45 s will label some sessions that previously passed; on this fleet the isolation test on healthy units completes in seconds, so the extra catch is likely genuine, but validate the 40-60 s band against known-good sessions before committing. If that band turns out populated, keep the 60 s hard label and carry 45 s as a warning feature only.
- Do not lower the COMM_FREEZE threshold from 5 s to the standard's 250 ms. Keep 5 s as the label, and add the 250 ms breach count and the 25 ms performance-time ratio as graded features instead.
  - today: 5 s stall in delivery = COMM_FREEZE.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout for CurrentDemandReq is 0,25 s and the SECC performance time for CurrentDemandRes is 0,025 s - by the letter of the standard, a conformant EV abandons the session after 250 ms of silence.
  - risk: This is a deliberate refusal to follow the standard's number. Applying 250 ms as a label threshold on real captures would fire on ordinary jitter, on PLC retransmission, and on capture artefacts, flooding the positive class. The graded feature preserves the diagnostic value - a median CurrentDemandRes latency drifting from 25 ms toward 250 ms is the leading indicator of an SECC control-loop failure - without corrupting the labels.
- Stop labelling EVErrorCode == 'NoData' as a fault, and attribute all remaining non-NO_ERROR EVErrorCodes to the vehicle rather than to the station; additionally treat a persistent EVReady==FALSE as an equivalent incompatibility signal.
  - today: EVErrorCode != NO_ERROR is a fault, which sweeps in NoData; and EVReady is not used, so half of the incompatibility aborts are missed.
  - under ISO: NoData means only that the vehicle has not yet determined its operating state and is legitimate in the earliest DC requests. EVErrorCodes are informational and must not influence the EVSE's behaviour. Table 100 makes FAILED_ChargingSystemIncompatibility optional and names EVReady=FALSE as the permitted alternative signalling.
  - risk: Low on the NoData half (a clear false-positive fix). The EVReady half adds a new detection and could over-fire, because EVReady=FALSE is entirely normal during CableCheck and PreCharge - only a FALSE that persists through PowerDeliveryReq(Start), or a TRUE->FALSE transition inside the CurrentDemand loop, should count.
- Exclude EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent from fault labelling entirely, and downgrade EVSE_Shutdown / EVSE_EmergencyShutdown when the session nevertheless completes the normal shutdown chain (PowerDeliveryReq(Stop) with ChargingComplete true, then SessionStopRes OK). Keep EVSE_Malfunction as a fault.
  - today: EVSE_Malfunction and EVSE_EmergencyShutdown are labelled faults; Shutdown and UtilityInterruptEvent are apparently not used.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-880] makes all DC_EVSEStatusCodes without explicit requirements informational; they shall not influence the charging process, and the forced-shutdown path is the control pilot, not this field. EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent specifically means the utility or operator requested a temporary load reduction - deliberate curtailment, which on a dual-connector site is exactly the load-sharing case that must not be scored as equipment failure. EVSE_Shutdown covers a customer-initiated stop.
  - risk: Moderate on the EmergencyShutdown downgrade: an E-Stop press is a real event even when the shutdown is orderly. Recommend downgrading to a separate OPERATOR_STOP class rather than to clean, so the event is still retrievable. The UtilityInterruptEvent exclusion is low risk and directly addresses a known false-positive source on this fleet.
- Re-base SLAC_FAILURE on the SDP/communication-setup evidence rather than on 'SLAC frames present but no V2G': require either an SDP request burst with no SDP response, or more than 20 s from the first SDP request to a SessionSetupRes that never arrives, before labelling.
  - today: SLAC without V2G = SLAC_FAILURE.
  - under ISO: The standard's own observable failure signatures for this phase are SDP-side: the client waits at least 250 ms per request, stops after 50 unanswered requests, and the EVCC must stop the session if no SessionSetupRes arrives within the 20 s communication-setup timeout while the SECC tears down at 18 s.
  - risk: Moderate but necessary. The powerline is a shared medium and SLAC frames from the neighbouring connector are captured on this one, so 'SLAC without V2G' mislabels a neighbour's handshake as this connector's failure. The trade is that a link that fails before SDP ever starts produces no SDP rows and would no longer be labelled - keep a separate low-severity NO_SETUP_ACTIVITY class for that case rather than reusing SLAC_FAILURE.
- Add an ONGOING_TIMEOUT label for ChargeParameterDiscovery and CableCheck Ongoing runs exceeding ~65 s from the FIRST Ongoing response, but deliberately do NOT add it for ContractAuthentication.
  - today: No Ongoing-timeout label at all; a session stuck polling ChargeParameterDiscovery for minutes is only caught, if at all, by the generic no-SessionStopRes rule.
  - under ISO: The EVCC must stop the session at 60 s of Ongoing without Finished, and the SECC must itself emit FAILED and stop at 55 s. The timer is anchored at the first Ongoing of the run, so a detector that resets on each poll never fires.
  - risk: The asymmetry is the important part. In an EIM/ExternalPayment DIN session the charger signals 'waiting for the driver to present a card' with plain Ongoing on ContractAuthenticationRes - DIN lacks the ISO Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction literal - so a long authorization loop is usually a human, and labelling it would manufacture faults out of ordinary customer behaviour. ChargeParameterDiscovery and CableCheck have no human in the loop and are safe.
- Treat isolation=='Invalid' persisting past the CableCheck phase (into PreCharge or CurrentDemand) as a fault, while continuing to treat Invalid during CableCheck and transient Warning as normal; and record No_IMD as a monitoring-blind-spot flag rather than as a clean isolation result.
  - today: Only isolation=='Fault' is labelled; Invalid and No_IMD are treated as non-events.
  - under ISO: Invalid means no isolation test has been carried out - it is the absence of a result, not a passing one. Reaching PowerDelivery or CurrentDemand having never seen Valid (and without No_IMD) means the isolation confirmation was skipped or failed. No_IMD means the charger has no insulation monitoring device, so it will never report Warning or Fault at all.
  - risk: Moderate: the element is optional in DC_EVSEStatus and many chargers omit it outside CableCheck, so 'field absent' must be handled as a distinct third state and must never be conflated with Invalid. Without that, every charger that simply stops sending the field would be labelled faulty.
- Do not label EVSENotification=='StopCharging' as a fault, and do not label the abrupt end of the CurrentDemand loop that follows it; label instead only the case where the EV keeps demanding current for an implausibly long time after the notification.
  - today: StopCharging is treated as a normal string on a user-initiated stop (already correct), but the delivery stall that follows it can still trip COMM_FREEZE and the missing-SessionStopRes rule.
  - under ISO: StopCharging plus NotificationMaxDelay is the standard EVSE-side stop signal; after that delay the SECC is explicitly permitted to stop charging unilaterally, for example by opening contactors - so the abrupt end is the documented consequence of a legitimate request, not an equipment fault.
  - risk: Low on suppression, but NotificationMaxDelay is not in the extracted schema, so the 'EV ignored the notification' half cannot be evaluated against the real deadline and must use a conservative fixed bound (e.g. 60 s). Do not tighten that bound without re-extracting the field.
- Do not label a mid-session ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq as a session restart when it is preceded by a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=Renegotiate, and do not label the CurrentDemand gap it creates as a delivery stall.
  - today: A mid-loop return to ChargeParameterDiscovery, and the CurrentDemand pause around it, would be caught by the 5 s stall rule and would look like a restart after failure.
  - under ISO: A renegotiation legitimately suspends the CurrentDemand loop while ChargeParameterDiscovery repeats, and the EVCC then returns with ChargeProgress=Start to apply the new limits. It is a defined mechanism, not a fault.
  - risk: Very low in practice because DIN DC sessions essentially never renegotiate, so this changes almost no labels. It is worth implementing anyway as a correctness guard, and any occurrence at all should be surfaced for inspection since ReNegotiation on a DIN link is itself unusual.
- Add a CLEAN-session positive definition rather than defining clean as the absence of triggers: a session is clean only if it reached PowerDeliveryReq(Stop) and terminated with SessionStopRes carrying an OK-prefixed ResponseCode, or ended at a capture boundary while otherwise conformant.
  - today: Clean is implicit - whatever no rule fired on.
  - under ISO: The session envelope is normative: a V2G communication session always starts with SessionSetupReq/Res and always ends with SessionStopReq/Res, and the DC shutdown chain runs PowerDeliveryReq(Stop) -> PowerDeliveryRes(OK) -> optional WeldingDetection -> SessionStopReq -> SessionStopRes.
  - risk: Low, and it mainly improves the negative class. The caveat is that WeldingDetection is optional and its absence must never be scored, and DIN SessionStopReq carries no ChargingSession element, so a detector that requires that element would reject every DIN session as unclean.

### not checkable from our capture

NOTIF-STOPCHARGING-MAXDELAY / ENUM-STOPCHARGING-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / FIELD-NOTIFICATIONMAXDELAY - NotificationMaxDelay (seconds) is not extracted; only the EVSENotification string is available, so the deadline for the EV's reaction cannot be evaluated, only heuristically bounded., NOTIF-RENEGOTIATION-MAXDELAY / ENUM-RENEGOTIATION-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / RENEG-EVSENOTIFICATION-MAXDELAY - same missing NotificationMaxDelay field., MSG-RECEIPTREQUIRED / FSM-CURRENTDEMANDRES-RECEIPT-NEXT / SEQ-DC-CURRENTDEMAND-RECEIPTREQUIRED / SEQ-RECEIPTREQUIRED-LOOP / RECEIPTREQUIRED-PER-IDENTIFICATION-MODE - ReceiptRequired boolean is not extracted, and MeteringReceiptReq/Res are not in the msg enumeration, so the metering-receipt branch of the DC loop cannot be checked., RC-FAILED-CHARGINGPROFILEINVALID / RC-FAILED-TARIFFSELECTIONINVALID / SEQ-CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-LIMITS / CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-SASCHEDULE / SEQ-EVSE-MUST-FULFIL-PROFILE / SEQ-RENEG-PROFILE-CHOICE / CHARGINGPROFILE-MAX-24-ENTRIES / PERF-CHARGINGPROFILE-SLOT-120S - no ChargingProfile, ChargingProfileEntryStart or ChargingProfileEntryMaxPower fields., SEQ-UNIQUE-SASCHEDULETUPLEID / SASCHEDULETUPLEID-UNIQUE / ELEC-SASCHEDULETUPLEID-RANGE / SCHED-ENTRY-COUNT-LIMITS / SCHED-RELATIVE-TIME-SEMANTICS / MSG-SASCHEDULE-ENTRY-COUNTS / MSG-CHARGEPARAMDISCOVERYRES-SASCHEDULE-OMISSION - no SAScheduleTupleID, SAScheduleList, PMaxScheduleEntry or MaxEntriesSAScheduleTuple fields, so the 'Finished CPD with no SASchedule' violation and all schedule cross-checks are unavailable., PAYMENTSERVICESELECTION-RESPONSECODES / RC-FAILED-PAYMENTSELECTIONINVALID / RC-FAILED-SERVICESELECTIONINVALID / RC-FAILED-NOCHARGESERVICESELECTED / SVC-SERVICEID-RANGE-AND-SELECTION - only extra.ServiceCategory is extracted; PaymentOptionList, SelectedPaymentOption, ServiceList and SelectedServiceList/ServiceID are missing, so only the resulting ResponseCode can be observed, never the cross-message mismatch that caused it., MSGSET-SELECTION-BY-PAYMENT-AND-MODE - SelectedPaymentOption and RequestedEnergyTransferMode/EnergyTransferType are not extracted, so the message set cannot be derived and message-set-conditional mandatory/optional checks (MSGSET-MANDATORY-OPTIONAL-MARKINGS) cannot run., RC-FAILED-WRONGENERGYTRANSFERMODE / ABNORM-CHARGEPARAM-SPECIFIC-FAILURES (root-cause half) - RequestedEnergyTransferMode and the AC/DC_EVChargeParameter discriminator are absent; the ResponseCode is visible but the parameter mismatch that triggered it is not., All CP-* rules (CP-DC-EV-STATE-CD-BEFORE-CABLECHECK, CP-DC-EV-STATE-B-AFTER-STOP, CP-DC-EV-NO-STATE-CHANGE-DURING-LOOP, CP-DC-EVSE-5PCT-DUTY-WHOLE-SESSION, CP-EVCC-STATE-E-F-ABORT, CP-STATE-EF-STOPS-SESSION, DC-CP-STATE-HOLD, AC-CP-TIMING-250MS, CP-AC-*) - IEC 61851-1 control pilot state and PWM duty cycle are not in the capture; only their message-level consequences (a FAILED CableCheckRes, a FAILED WeldingDetectionRes/SessionStopRes) are observable., All contactor rules (CP-AC-POWERDELIVERYRES-FAILED-CONTACTORERROR-START/STOP, AC-SECC-CONTACTOR-OK-PATHS, AC-SECC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-CONSTRAINTS, ELEC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-TIMING, RC-CONTACTOR-FAILED-ON-START/STOP) - contactor state is not observable, and FAILED_ContactorError is an ISO literal absent from the DIN responseCodeType; only the PowerDeliveryRes latency proxy is available., All TLS and certificate rules (TLS-VERSION-AND-ROLE, TLS-CIPHER-SUITES, TLS-ABORT-ON-CERT-VERIFICATION-FAILURE, TLS-OFF-RESTRICTS-TO-EIM, SEC-TLS-DOWNGRADE-PROHIBITED, CERT-SIZE-DEPTH-VALIDITY, SEC-OCSP-AND-PAIRING-CONSTANTS, RC-FAILED-CERT*, RC-FAILED-SIGNATUREERROR, MSG-CERTIFICATEUPDATERES-RETRYCOUNTER) - DIN 70121 carries no TLS or certificates and none of these fields are extracted., MSG-AUTHORIZATIONREQ-GENCHALLENGE / RC-FAILED-CHALLENGEINVALID / SEQ-AUTHORIZATION-GENCHALLENGE - GenChallenge bytes are not extracted, so the 16-byte length check and the cross-session RNG-reuse check cannot be run., SDP payload rules (SDP-REQ-PAYLOAD-TYPE-AND-LENGTH, SDP-RES-PAYLOAD-TYPE-AND-LENGTH, SDP-SECURITY-TRANSPORT-ENCODING, SDP-OPTION-TCP-MIRROR, SDP-OPTION-TLS-MIRROR/FALLBACK, SDP-REQUEST-FORMAT-AND-PORTS, SDP-RESPONSE-UNICAST-BACK-TO-CLIENT, SDP-REQ-MULTICAST-DEST, SDP-UDP-PORTS) - only an SDP info line is extracted, not the 0x9000/0x9001 payload bytes, ports, addresses or option bytes., V2GTP framing rules (V2GTP-HEADER-STRUCTURE, V2GTP-VERSION-AND-INVERSE, V2GTP-PAYLOAD-TYPES, V2GTP-ONE-V2G-MSG-PER-PDU, V2GTP-HEADER-CHECK-ORDER-AND-DISCARD, V2GTP-TCP-PORTS-DYNAMIC-RANGE, V2GTP-USE-PORT-FROM-LAST-SDP-RESPONSE) - no raw bytes, no TCP port numbers, no payload-length field in the schema., EXI encoding rules (EXI-HEADER-NO-COOKIE-DISCARD-NONCONFORMING, EXI-OPTION-VALUEPARTITIONCAPACITY-0, EXI-PROFILE-BUILTIN-GRAMMARS-ZERO, EXI-SCHEMA-DEVIATION-HANDLING) - only the coarse Wireshark validation verdict is available, not the EXI header bits or coder options., IP/transport stack rules (IPV6-NO-FRAGMENTATION, IPV6-SLAAC-LINKLOCAL, ICMPV6-MANDATORY-SET, TCP-NO-URGENT-POINTER, UDP-RFC768-MANDATORY, NET-DNS-AND-DHCP-OPTIONS, STATE-EVCC-DLINK-TO-IPASSIGN) - no IPv6/ICMPv6/DHCPv6 events and no TCP flag fields; kind=='tcp' exposes only RST/RETX/LOST., SEQ-EVCC-TERMINATE-CONNECTION-AFTER-STOP / CONN-SECC-TERMINATE-AFTER-SESSION-STOP / CONN-EVCC-CLOSE-AFTER-SESSIONSTOPRES (graceful-close half) - TCP FIN is not represented in the kind=='tcp' msg enumeration (RST only), so 'connection left open after SessionStopRes' and 'clean FIN after SessionStopRes' are both unobservable; only the RST-before-SessionStopRes direction is checkable., TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-START-ON-DLINK-READY / SECC-COMMSETUP-18S-ACTION / STATE-*-DLINK-* - the ISO 15118-3 D-LINK_READY / D-LINK_TERMINATE / D-LINK_PAUSE primitives are not captured; the communication-setup t0 can only be approximated by the first SDP row, and on a shared powerline HPAV/SLAC rows may belong to the neighbouring connector so they are not a safe anchor., MSG-METERINGRECEIPTREQ-ECHO / RC-FAILED-METERINGSIGNATURENOTVALID / ELEC-METERREADING-RANGE - MeterInfo/MeterReading fields and the MeteringReceipt message pair are not extracted., SEQ-RESUME-EVCC-PARAMS / SEQ-RESUME-SECC-PARAMS / CONN-DLINK-PAUSE-ON-CHARGINGSESSION-PAUSE - extra.ChargingSession is present, but DIN 70121 SessionStopReq has no ChargingSession element and DIN has no pause/resume, so these ISO resume-parameter cross-checks have nothing to compare against; a ChargingSession value appearing on a DIN link should instead be flagged as a dialect anomaly., ELEC-EVSECURRENTREGULATIONTOLERANCE-based current-tracking band - EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance is not in the schema (only EVSEPeakCurrentRipple appears in extra), so the standards-traceable regulation band cannot be constructed and any present-vs-target current tolerance must be documented as a heuristic per ELEC-PRESENT-VS-TARGET-NO-NUMERIC-TOLERANCE.

## lens: electrical

### proposed features (34)

- **evse_status_sev_held** (high) — Severity of the last DC_EVSEStatusCode seen, held between events, with FAILED-response rows excluded because their payload is arbitrary filler.
  - compute: Maintain last_status. On each v2g event: if resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED') -> do NOT update last_status (V2G2-736 filler) and emit the held value; else if evse_status non-empty -> last_status = evse_status. sev = {EVSE_Ready:0.0, EVSE_IsolationMonitoringActive:0.0, EVSE_NotReady:0.1, EVSE_Shutdown:0.3, EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent:0.6, EVSE_EmergencyShutdown:1.0, EVSE_Malfunction:1.0}.get(last_status, 0.0). Emit sev.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 for the whole session except a trailing 0.3 (EVSE_Shutdown) at a user/charger-initiated stop.
  - FP risk: EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent is deliberate curtailment and EVSE_Shutdown is the normal end-of-charge announcement; both must stay features and never alarm alone.
- **evse_status_notready_in_loop_s** (medium) — Seconds the charger has reported EVSE_NotReady while the DC charging loop is running - the charger contradicting itself about being ready to deliver energy.
  - compute: in_loop becomes True at the first CurrentDemandReq after a PowerDeliveryReq whose ChargeProgress=='Start' (DIN: extra.ReadyToChargeState=='true'); False at PowerDeliveryReq Stop. Track notready_t0: set to t on the first event with held status EVSE_NotReady while in_loop, cleared when held status changes. Emit (t - notready_t0) if set else 0.
  - clean looks like: 0 s - the expected in-loop values are EVSE_Ready and EVSE_IsolationMonitoringActive.
  - FP risk: Firmware that leaves the field stale at NotReady for the first one or two loop messages; require >=2 s before it carries weight.
- **evse_status_regress_flag** (high) — Charger status fell back from Ready/IsolationMonitoringActive to NotReady, Shutdown, EmergencyShutdown or Malfunction while the CurrentDemand loop was still running.
  - compute: Track seen_ready (held status was ever EVSE_Ready) and in_loop as above. Emit 1.0 on the first event where in_loop and seen_ready and held status in {EVSE_NotReady, EVSE_Shutdown, EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent, EVSE_EmergencyShutdown, EVSE_Malfunction}; latch at 1.0 thereafter for the session.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 until the closing PowerDelivery(Stop); a Shutdown that arrives after the loop has been left is not a regression.
  - FP risk: EVSE_Shutdown on a user stop can land on the last CurrentDemandRes before the Stop request; suppress when a StopCharging notification or ChargingComplete=true preceded it.
- **evse_shutdown_unannounced** (high) — Charger announced Shutdown/EmergencyShutdown mid-charge with no preceding StopCharging notification and no ChargingComplete - an unannounced charger-side stop, as opposed to the normal announced one.
  - compute: Track seen_stopcharging (any row with notification=='StopCharging'), seen_complete (any CurrentDemandReq with charge_complete=='true'). Emit 1.0 at the first event with held status in {EVSE_Shutdown, EVSE_EmergencyShutdown} while in_loop and not seen_stopcharging and not seen_complete; latch.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 - on this fleet a user stop always shows StopCharging -> EVSE_Shutdown -> graceful SessionStop.
  - FP risk: A charger that opens contactors without ever setting EVSENotification is out of spec but the session may still end cleanly; keep as feature, not alarm.
- **isolation_state_sev** (high) — Severity of the last EVSEIsolationStatus, treating Invalid as the normal pre-measurement state, Warning as transient, only Fault as hard.
  - compute: Hold last non-empty isolation from rows whose resp_code is not FAILED-prefixed. sev = {Invalid:0.0, No_IMD:0.0, Valid:0.0, Warning:0.4, Fault:1.0}.get(last,0.0).
  - clean looks like: 0.0, with occasional transient 0.4 during CableCheck.
  - FP risk: Warning appears transiently in healthy fleet sessions - never treat 0.4 as a fault, only as a trend input.
- **isolation_warning_run_s** (medium) — Duration of the current uninterrupted run of EVSEIsolationStatus=='Warning', plus how many Warning runs the session has had - a degrading-insulation trend that is not yet a Fault.
  - compute: On each non-FAILED v2g row carrying isolation: if 'Warning' and warn_t0 is None -> warn_t0=t, n_warn_runs+=1; if isolation != 'Warning' -> warn_t0=None. Emit run = (t-warn_t0) if warn_t0 else 0.0, and n_warn_runs as a second scalar.
  - clean looks like: run < ~2 s, n_warn_runs <= 1.
  - FP risk: Transient Warning is explicitly normal on this fleet; only a long sustained run or repeated runs across sessions on one connector is meaningful.
- **isolation_unresolved_at_precharge** (high) — The cable check was left behind without the isolation measurement ever reporting Valid (and without the charger declaring No_IMD) - the isolation test was skipped or never produced a result.
  - compute: Track seen_isolation_field (any non-empty isolation on a non-FAILED row), seen_valid (isolation=='Valid'), seen_no_imd (isolation=='No_IMD'). At the first PreChargeReq of the dialog emit 1.0 if seen_isolation_field and not seen_valid and not seen_no_imd, else 0.0; latch for the rest of the session.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 - a healthy DC session reaches Valid during CableCheck.
  - FP risk: EVSEIsolationStatus is optional (minOccurs=0); the seen_isolation_field guard is essential or every charger that omits it looks faulty.
- **isolation_invalid_in_charge_loop** (medium) — Fraction of CurrentDemandRes messages that still report EVSEIsolationStatus=='Invalid' after the cable check completed - the charger delivering power with no live isolation result.
  - compute: After cablecheck_end (first CableCheckRes with OK+Finished, or the first PreChargeReq), count n_cd_res with isolation field present and n_cd_res_invalid where isolation=='Invalid'. Emit n_cd_res_invalid/max(n_cd_res,1).
  - clean looks like: 0.0.
  - FP risk: Some firmware simply freezes the field after the test; correlate with whether it ever showed Valid at all before scoring.
- **isolation_monitoring_blind** (low) — Charger declares No_IMD: it has no insulation monitoring device, so isolation faults can never be reported on this connector.
  - compute: Latch 1.0 the first time a non-FAILED row carries isolation=='No_IMD'.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 on an IMD-equipped charger; 1.0 is a station capability statement, not a fault.
  - FP risk: Must never be scored as a fault; its purpose is to tell the model that isolation features carry no information for this session.
- **ev_err_charger_attributed** (high) — The vehicle stopped because it judged the charger unable to regulate - DC_EVErrorCode FAILED_ChargingCurrentdifferential or FAILED_ChargingVoltageOutOfRange.
  - compute: On a non-FAILED-response v2g row: emit 1.0 if ev_err in {FAILED_ChargingCurrentdifferential, FAILED_ChargingVoltageOutOfRange}; latch for the session.
  - clean looks like: 0.0; expected value of EVErrorCode is NO_ERROR.
  - FP risk: Very low - these two literals are explicit vehicle-side reports of a charger regulation failure. Pair with the measured tracking error to confirm attribution.
- **ev_err_vehicle_attributed** (high) — Vehicle-side DC_EVErrorCode other than NO_ERROR/NoData and other than the two charger-accusing codes (RESS temperature, shift position, connector lock, RESS malfunction, system incompatibility, Reserved_*).
  - compute: On a non-FAILED-response row: e = ev_err; emit 1.0 if e not in {'', NO_ERROR, NoData} and e not in the charger-attributed pair and not e.startswith('Reserved_'); emit 0.5 if e.startswith('Reserved_') (non-conformant vendor code). Latch the maximum.
  - clean looks like: 0.0.
  - FP risk: NoData is normal in the first requests of a session and must be excluded; Reserved_* is an implementation defect, not a charging fault.
- **ev_err_nodata_late** (low) — EVErrorCode still 'NoData' once the session has reached PreCharge or CurrentDemand - the vehicle never resolved its own operating state.
  - compute: Latch 1.0 on the first PreChargeReq/CurrentDemandReq whose ev_err=='NoData'.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 - NoData is legitimate only in the earliest DC requests.
  - FP risk: Low; some EVCC stacks are simply lazy about the field, so keep it a feature.
- **ev_ready_false_run_s** (high) — How long EVReady has been false while the DC charging loop is running and current is actually flowing - a contradiction, and the licensed alternative signalling for charging-system incompatibility.
  - compute: On CurrentDemandReq rows with ev_ready non-empty: if ev_ready=='false' and ready_false_t0 is None -> ready_false_t0=t; if 'true' -> ready_false_t0=None. Emit (t-ready_false_t0) if set and in_loop else 0. Also maintain ev_ready_flap_count = number of true->false transitions after the loop opened.
  - clean looks like: 0 s in the loop; EVReady==false during CableCheck/PreCharge is normal and must not be counted.
  - FP risk: Field may be absent or stale on some EVCCs; gate on ev_ready being populated. Also do not count the pre-loop phases where false is expected.
- **charge_complete_suppressor** (high) — ChargingComplete / BulkChargingComplete state - the benign-termination markers that explain a current taper or an abrupt end of the loop.
  - compute: Latch cc = 1.0 on any CurrentDemandReq (or PowerDeliveryReq DC_EVPowerDeliveryParameter) with charge_complete=='true'; bulk = 1.0 on bulk_complete=='true'. Emit both plus soc (last EVRESSSOC).
  - clean looks like: cc goes 1.0 shortly before the closing PowerDelivery(Stop) on a full charge; may stay 0.0 on a user stop.
  - FP risk: None as a feature; its whole job is to suppress false shortfall/stall alarms. BulkChargingComplete is optional and its absence must not be read as false.
- **stopcharging_age_s** (high) — Seconds since the first EVSENotification=='StopCharging', and whether the EV has answered it with a PowerDelivery(Stop) yet.
  - compute: Latch stop_t0 = t at the first non-FAILED row with notification=='StopCharging'. Emit age = (t-stop_t0) if stop_t0 and no PowerDeliveryReq(Stop) has been seen since, else 0. Secondary scalar answered = 1.0 once a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=='Stop' (DIN: ReadyToChargeState=='false') follows stop_t0.
  - clean looks like: age of a few seconds then answered=1.0 - StopCharging followed by an orderly stop is a NORMAL operator/user termination.
  - FP risk: High if StopCharging itself is treated as a fault - it is not. Only a large unanswered age (EV ignoring the request) is evidence, and even then the charger, not the EV, usually ends the session.
- **renegotiation_unanswered_s** (low) — Seconds since the charger set EVSENotification=='ReNegotiation' without the EV starting a renegotiation - and, on DIN, the mere presence of the value, which the DIN dialect gives the EV no way to satisfy.
  - compute: Latch reneg_t0 at the first non-FAILED row with notification=='ReNegotiation'; clear on a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=='Renegotiate' or on the next ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq. Emit (t-reneg_t0) if set else 0, plus a latched flag reneg_seen.
  - clean looks like: 0 - ReNegotiation is essentially never used on DIN DC and its appearance is itself anomalous.
  - FP risk: Low frequency; main risk is confusing the EVSENotification literal 'ReNegotiation' with the ChargeProgress literal 'Renegotiate'.
- **ongoing_ratio_by_phase** (high) — Age of the current EVSEProcessing=='Ongoing' run, anchored at the FIRST Ongoing of that phase, kept separately for ContractAuthentication, ChargeParameterDiscovery and CableCheck because their benign causes differ.
  - compute: Per phase p in {ContractAuthentication, ChargeParameterDiscovery, CableCheck}: on a Res of that phase with evse_processing=='Ongoing' and ongoing_t0[p] is None -> ongoing_t0[p]=t; on 'Finished' -> ongoing_t0[p]=None. Emit three ratios (t-ongoing_t0[p])/60.0 (0 when None) and a boolean perf_breach[p] = span>=55.
  - clean looks like: < 0.1 for CableCheck/ChargeParameterDiscovery; ContractAuthentication may legitimately run tens of seconds while the driver authorises.
  - FP risk: Authorization Ongoing is a wait-on-human state (ISO makes this explicit with Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction, which DIN lacks) - it must not be scored like a stuck cable check. Reset t0 on a re-dialog.
- **cablecheck_elapsed_ratio** (high) — Elapsed cable-check time as a fraction of the 40 s EVCC timeout, with the 38 s SECC performance time as a second, softer ratio.
  - compute: cc_t0 = t of the first CableCheckReq of the current dialog (reset on a new supportedAppProtocolReq/SessionSetupReq). cc_end = t of the first CableCheckRes with rc OK and evse_processing=='Finished', or failing that the first PreChargeReq (which per [V2G2-525] proves the check finished). Emit (min(t,cc_end or t)-cc_t0)/40.0 and .../38.0; freeze once cc_end is set.
  - clean looks like: peak well below 1.0, typically 0.1-0.5.
  - FP risk: A ring-file seam inside the cable-check phase inflates the span; gate on the largest gap between consecutive events in the window being < 10 s.
- **precharge_elapsed_and_convergence** (high) — Pre-charge phase age against the 7 s EVCC timeout together with how well EVSEPresentVoltage is converging on EVTargetVoltage - the pair that separates a slow-but-working ramp from a charger that cannot reach the pack voltage.
  - compute: pc_t0 = first PreChargeReq of the dialog; pc_done at the first PowerDeliveryReq. ratio = (t-pc_t0)/7.0 while not done. Keep gap = |ev_target_v - evse_v| from the latest PreChargeReq/PreChargeRes pair, gap0 = gap at ratio>=0.3, and slope = (gap0-gap)/max(t-t_at_gap0, 0.5). Emit ratio, gap/max(20.0, 0.05*ev_target_v), and a converging flag = slope > 0.05*gap0.
  - clean looks like: ratio peaks around 0.2-0.8; gap ratio falls monotonically toward 0; converging=1.
  - FP risk: Every pre-charge starts with a huge gap (0 V to pack voltage) so the gap alone is meaningless before ~30 % of the timer; the standard publishes no numeric voltage tolerance, so the 20 V / 5 % band is a documented heuristic.
- **unexplained_current_shortfall_run_s** (high) — Consecutive seconds in which delivered current sits materially below EVTargetCurrent while the charger declares NO limit reached and nothing benign explains it - the precise precondition the standard gives for FAILED_ChargingCurrentdifferential, and the only form of under-delivery that is not routine load sharing.
  - compute: On each CurrentDemandRes paired with its CurrentDemandReq: band = 0.10*ev_target_i + 0.5*ripple where ripple = float(extra.EVSEPeakCurrentRipple) if present else 0 (peak-to-peak, hence /2). shortfall = ev_target_i - evse_i. unexplained = shortfall > band AND limit_achieved=='' (no v/c/p flag) AND ev_target_i >= max(5.0, extra.EVSEMinimumCurrentLimit or 0) AND notification != 'StopCharging' AND charge_complete != 'true' AND bulk_complete != 'true' AND held evse_status != 'EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent' AND evse_max_i (if present) > ev_target_i. If unexplained and run_t0 is None -> run_t0=t; if not unexplained -> run_t0=None. Emit (t-run_t0) if set else 0, plus the instantaneous shortfall/max(ev_target_i,1).
  - clean looks like: 0 s. Deep shortfalls WITH a limit flag, with UtilityInterruptEvent, or during taper are normal load sharing / curtailment and score 0 here.
  - FP risk: HIGHEST-risk feature in the set. Dual-connector load sharing routinely halves delivered current; the fleet has been observed sharing WITHOUT EVSECurrentLimitAchieved set, so the limit-flag gate alone is not sufficient - require a long run (>=15-30 s) before it carries weight, and never alarm on it standalone. EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance is not in the schema, so the 10 % band is heuristic and must be documented as such.
- **limit_flag_state_and_inconsistency** (medium) — Which of EVSEVoltage/Current/PowerLimitAchieved are set, and whether a set flag is contradicted by the delivered value sitting far below the charger's own declared maximum.
  - compute: flags = limit_achieved string: emit three booleans f_v='v' in s, f_c='c' in s, f_p='p' in s. inconsistency = 1.0 when f_c and evse_max_i and evse_i < 0.8*evse_max_i, or f_v and evse_max_v and evse_v < 0.8*evse_max_v; else 0.
  - clean looks like: flags may legitimately be set for long stretches (that is the charger honestly declaring saturation); inconsistency 0.0.
  - FP risk: An empty limit_achieved string means either all-false or the fields were absent; treat 'no flag' as weak evidence only. Mid-session drops in evse_max_i with f_c set are curtailment, not a defect.
- **delivered_current_zero_run_s** (high) — Consecutive seconds inside the charging loop with essentially zero delivered current while the EV is demanding a real current and no limit flag, notification or completion explains it - the observable proxy for a contactor that never closed or a power stage that will not start.
  - compute: Same gating as unexplained_current_shortfall_run_s, but the condition is evse_i <= max(1.0, 0.02*ev_target_i) with ev_target_i >= 5 A. Maintain zero_t0 the same way; emit (t-zero_t0) or 0. Also emit v_present = 1.0 if evse_v >= 0.8*ev_target_v (output voltage there but no current) to separate 'contactor open' from 'no output at all'.
  - clean looks like: 0 s once the loop opens; a healthy loop ramps current within a second or two of PowerDeliveryRes(OK).
  - FP risk: An extreme load-sharing case could in principle drive one connector to near zero, and the first one or two CurrentDemand exchanges legitimately show zero while the ramp starts - require >=5 s. Keep as feature, not alarm.
- **current_before_powerdelivery_start** (medium) — Non-trivial output current observed before the charging loop was authorised by a PowerDeliveryReq(Start) - energy flowing ahead of the contactor command.
  - compute: Before the first PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=='Start' (DIN: ReadyToChargeState=='true'), emit 1.0 on any PreChargeRes/CableCheckRes with evse_i is not None and evse_i > 5.0; latch.
  - clean looks like: 0.0 - pre-charge draws only a small capacitive current.
  - FP risk: Pre-charge inrush into the vehicle's DC-link can momentarily exceed a few amps; the 5 A threshold is heuristic and should be raised if the fleet shows benign inrush.
- **current_at_stop_request** (medium) — Delivered current still flowing at the moment the EV asks to stop - the EV was required to have ramped to zero before sending PowerDeliveryReq(Stop).
  - compute: At a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress=='Stop' (DIN ReadyToChargeState=='false'), emit last_evse_i / max(last_ev_target_i_at_loop_peak, 1.0) using the most recent CurrentDemandRes/Req before it; 0 otherwise. Also emit absolute last_evse_i/50.
  - clean looks like: ~0 - a clean end of charge tapers first.
  - FP risk: An abrupt but legitimate EV-side stop (driver pressed stop in the car) also shows current at the Stop; use it as severity context, not as a fault by itself.
- **voltage_tracking_error_frac** (high) — Relative error between EVSEPresentVoltage and EVTargetVoltage inside the charging loop, plus the sustained-excursion duration - the objective evidence behind FAILED_ChargingVoltageOutOfRange.
  - compute: On CurrentDemandRes paired with its Req, with both voltages present and inside Table 68 ranges: err = |evse_v - ev_target_v| / max(ev_target_v, 1.0). If err > 0.05 and vt0 is None -> vt0=t; if err <= 0.05 -> vt0=None. Emit err and (t-vt0) or 0.
  - clean looks like: err < 0.02; run 0 s.
  - FP risk: ISO publishes NO numeric present-vs-target voltage tolerance (it is delegated to IEC 61851-23), so the 5 % band is explicitly a heuristic. Transient error during a current step is normal.
- **voltage_over_declared_limit_frac** (high) — How far the delivered output voltage exceeds the tighter of the EV's declared EVMaximumVoltageLimit and the charger's own EVSEMaximumVoltageLimit.
  - compute: lim = min(x for x in (ev_max_v_held, evse_max_v_held) if x); emit (evse_v/lim - 1.0) when evse_v and lim are present and both inside Table 68 (<=1000 V), else 0. Hold the two maxima from the latest non-FAILED message that carried them. Track n_consecutive samples with the value > 0.02.
  - clean looks like: <= 0.0 (delivered voltage at or below both declared maxima).
  - FP risk: A stale or mis-scaled ev_max_v produces phantom over-voltage; range-check first and require several consecutive samples. Never compute it from a FAILED response.
- **param_incompatibility_overlap** (medium) — The EV's and charger's declared operating envelopes do not overlap, computed as soon as ChargeParameterDiscovery has exchanged both sides - predicts an incompatibility abort before any current flows.
  - compute: After the first ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes with evse_max_v and the ChargeParameterDiscoveryReq's ev_max_v: emit 1.0 if ev_max_v > evse_max_v (pack needs more than the charger can give) or if extra.EVSEMinimumVoltageLimit is present and float(it) > ev_max_v (pack below the charger's accurate range) or if extra.EVSEMinimumCurrentLimit > ev_max_i. Latch.
  - clean looks like: 0.0.
  - FP risk: Vehicles routinely declare a max voltage above what they will actually request; the overlap test should compare against the later EVTargetVoltage as well before being taken as causal.
- **ev_rampdown_lag_after_limit_drop_s** (medium) — Seconds since the charger reduced its advertised limit (or asked for StopCharging) during which the EV is still demanding more than 110 % of the new limit - the standard's 5 s / 10 % condition that makes a charger-side drop lawful and attributable to the vehicle.
  - compute: Hold prev_evse_max_i. On a CurrentDemandRes where evse_max_i is present and evse_max_i < 0.9*prev_evse_max_i -> drop_t0=t, new_lim=evse_max_i (also set drop_t0 at the first StopCharging with new_lim=0). While drop_t0 set: if ev_target_i > 1.10*new_lim emit (t-drop_t0) else clear drop_t0 and emit 0.
  - clean looks like: < 5 s - the EV is required to follow a falling edge within 5 s.
  - FP risk: The 5 s/10 % figures are ISO; DIN publishes no equivalent, so treat >5 s as attribution evidence (the charger was entitled to drop the EV) rather than as a charger fault.
- **welding_residual_voltage** (medium) — Output voltage still present, and not decaying, across the WeldingDetection phase - the classic welded-contactor signature.
  - compute: On WeldingDetectionRes with evse_v present and rc not FAILED: emit v_frac = evse_v / max(last_loop_ev_target_v, 1.0) and decay = (first_wd_v - evse_v)/max(t - first_wd_t, 0.5). Latch max v_frac over the phase and the number of WeldingDetectionRes seen.
  - clean looks like: v_frac decaying toward 0 across the phase; a high first sample is normal (DC-link discharge).
  - FP risk: The EVSE output filter legitimately holds charge for seconds - only a sustained, non-decaying voltage over the whole phase is meaningful, which is why this is a feature and not a hard rule.
- **soc_progress_stall** (low) — State of charge failing to rise while substantial current is being delivered, or jumping implausibly - an energy-transfer or BMS-reporting inconsistency.
  - compute: On CurrentDemandReq with soc present: keep (t_ref, soc_ref) from 120 s ago (ring buffer of (t,soc)). If evse_i has averaged > 10 A over that window and soc <= soc_ref -> stall_flag=1 else 0. jump_flag = 1 if |soc - soc_prev| > 5 between consecutive messages. Emit both plus soc/100.
  - clean looks like: stall_flag 0 while charging; jump_flag 0; soc monotonically rising.
  - FP risk: SOC is reported in whole percent, so a flat SOC over 120 s at low current or near 100 % is normal - the >10 A gate and the long window are essential. Never evaluate near ChargingComplete.
- **remaining_time_rising** (low) — RemainingTimeToFullSoC trending upward instead of downward across the charging loop - the earliest quantitative sign of a charger under-delivering.
  - compute: Keep the value from 120 s ago; emit (remaining_full_min_now - remaining_full_min_120s_ago) normalised by 120 (in the field's own units), clipped at 0 below. Only evaluated while in_loop, charge_complete!='true' and the field is populated on consecutive messages.
  - clean looks like: negative (falling) - emitted feature 0.
  - FP risk: The column is named *_min but is populated from RemainingTimeToFullSoC, which ISO defines in SECONDS (0..172800) - confirm the unit before range-checking. Estimates re-baseline noisily right after a limit change, so require a sustained rise.
- **phys_range_violation_count** (high) — Running count of decoded physical values outside the standard's declared ranges - almost always a decoder/scaling fault rather than a real electrical excursion, and a prerequisite gate for every comparison-based rule.
  - compute: On every non-FAILED v2g row, increment for each of: evse_v/ev_target_v/ev_max_v/evse_max_v outside [0,1000]; evse_i/ev_target_i/ev_max_i/evse_max_i outside [0,400]; soc outside [0,100]. Emit the running count.
  - clean looks like: 0.
  - FP risk: None as a feature; its purpose is to invalidate the other electrical features for that session rather than to accuse the charger.
- **chargeprogress_state_machine** (high) — ChargeProgress / ReadyToChargeState state track: whether a Start has been seen, how many Starts without an intervening Stop, whether a Renegotiate preceded any Start, and whether the loop was left with a Stop at all.
  - compute: cp = extra.ChargeProgress if present else ('Start' if extra.ReadyToChargeState=='true' else 'Stop' if =='false' else None) on each PowerDeliveryReq. Emit: seen_start (latched), n_start_without_stop, reneg_before_start (1.0 if a Renegotiate appears with seen_start False), left_with_stop (latched at the first Stop after a Start).
  - clean looks like: seen_start 1, n_start_without_stop 1, reneg_before_start 0, left_with_stop 1 by the end of a clean charge.
  - FP risk: DIN carries ReadyToChargeState rather than ChargeProgress; a detector keyed only on ChargeProgress silently sees nothing on this fleet. A second Start after a renegotiation is legal and must not count as a duplicate.
- **close_sequence_completeness** (high) — How far the shutdown sequence got: PowerDelivery(Stop) -> [WeldingDetection]* -> SessionStopReq/Res, and whether the closing responses carried OK.
  - compute: Emit a monotone score: 0.0 nothing, 0.25 PowerDeliveryRes to a Stop with OK, 0.5 WeldingDetectionRes seen (optional stage), 0.75 SessionStopReq sent, 1.0 SessionStopRes with rc not FAILED. Separately emit failed_close = 1.0 if a SessionStopRes or WeldingDetectionRes carried a FAILED-prefixed rc.
  - clean looks like: 1.0 with failed_close 0.0.
  - FP risk: On this fleet a session very often ends at a ring-file boundary followed by hours of idle, which looks identical to a truncated close - do not treat a low score as a fault without corroboration (TCP RST, a FAILED response, or an unanswered request past its per-message timeout).

### proposed hard rules (16)

- `hr_isolation_fault` → ISOLATION_FAULT (confidence 0.97)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND not event.resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED') AND event.isolation=='Fault'
  - why: isolationLevelType 'Fault' means the measured isolation resistance is below the IEC 61851-23 fault level. It is the one isolation value that is unambiguously hard: 'Invalid' is the normal pre-measurement state and 'Warning' occurs transiently in healthy fleet sessions.
  - FP risk: Only from reading the field out of a FAILED response, where all mandatory parameters are arbitrary filler by [V2G2-736] - hence the resp_code guard. Also require the field to be non-empty rather than defaulting an absent optional element.
- `hr_isolation_fault_charging_continues` → ISOLATION_FAULT (confidence 0.99)
  - condition: hr_isolation_fault has fired at time tf AND at least one CurrentDemandReq is observed at t > tf in the same V2G dialog
  - why: Continuing energy transfer after the charger has reported an isolation Fault is a severe safety-conformance violation, not merely a fault indication. Escalates severity above the bare Fault report.
  - FP risk: None beyond hr_isolation_fault itself; the extra CurrentDemandReq must be in the same dialog (no intervening supportedAppProtocolReq/SessionSetupReq) so a retry session is not mis-attributed.
- `hr_evse_malfunction` → EVSE_FAULT (confidence 0.95)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND not resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED') AND event.evse_status=='EVSE_Malfunction'
  - why: Table 98 defines EVSE_Malfunction as a non-recoverable charger fault (example given: isolation failure). It has no benign reading.
