  - FP risk: [V2G2-880] makes status codes informational, so the code alone does not prove the session ended - the alarm is about charger health, and the fault time should be the first occurrence. Exclude FAILED-response rows.
- `hr_evse_emergency_shutdown` → EVSE_FAULT (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND not resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED') AND event.evse_status=='EVSE_EmergencyShutdown'
  - why: Covers E-Stop pressed at the station, charging-system incompatibility and emergency shutdown in progress. Any of the three is an incident worth an alarm.
  - FP risk: A deliberate operator E-Stop is 'correct' behaviour by a human, so the alarm is true-but-not-a-defect; downstream triage should separate it. Enforcement happens on the control pilot, not in the protocol, so the session may simply stop with no further evidence.
- `hr_response_code_failed` → PROTOCOL_FAILED (confidence 0.98)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND event.msg.endswith('Res') AND event.resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED')
  - why: Any ResponseCode with the FAILED prefix is a negative response; the EVCC must stop the session and the SECC must tear down the connection. It is the proximate cause of death of the session and the single highest-precision marker in the protocol.
  - FP risk: Matching must be case-insensitive on the prefix (the SAP schema spells its failure 'Failed_NoNegotiation'), and OK_* codes must never match. Everything else in that same message is filler and must not be scored.
- `hr_sap_no_negotiation` → INTEROP_FAILURE (confidence 0.99)
  - condition: event.msg=='supportedAppProtocolRes' AND event.resp_code.upper()=='FAILED_NONEGOTIATION'
  - why: The charger supports none of the application protocols the vehicle offered. The session cannot legally proceed to SessionSetup; this is the earliest and cleanest interoperability fault available.
  - FP risk: Only a spelling trap: the literal is CamelCase 'Failed_NoNegotiation', so compare upper-cased. If a SessionSetupReq nevertheless follows, that is an additional EVCC conformance violation worth recording.
- `hr_sequence_error` → PROTOCOL_FAILED (confidence 0.98)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND event.resp_code.upper()=='FAILED_SEQUENCEERROR'
  - why: The charger received a request it did not expect in its current wait state. It names the exact point where the two state machines diverged and identifies the offending preceding request.
  - FP risk: None on the code itself; the diagnosis (which request was illegal) depends on correct request/response pairing, which a ring-file seam can disturb.
- `hr_ev_error_code` → EV_ERROR (sub-attribute EVSE_REGULATION when ev_err in {FAILED_ChargingCurrentdifferential, FAILED_ChargingVoltageOutOfRange}) (confidence 0.95)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND event.msg.endswith('Req') AND not (paired response is FAILED) AND event.ev_err not in {'','NO_ERROR','NoData'} AND not event.ev_err.startswith('Reserved_')
  - why: DC_EVErrorCode other than NO_ERROR is the vehicle explicitly annunciating an internal or charger-caused fault. Two of the literals are explicit statements that the vehicle STOPPED the session because the station could not hold the requested current or kept the pack out of its voltage range - those must be attributed to the station, the rest to the vehicle.
  - FP risk: 'NoData' means the vehicle has not yet determined its operating state and is normal in early messages - it must be excluded (the current labeller does not exclude it). 'Reserved_A/B/C' are non-conformant vendor codes, an implementation defect rather than a charging fault. The EVSE is forbidden to react to EVErrorCode, so do not additionally flag the charger for 'ignoring' it.
- `hr_cablecheck_timeout_40s` → ISOLATION_FAULT / CABLECHECK_TIMEOUT (confidence 0.9)
  - condition: (t - t_first_CableCheckReq_of_dialog) >= 40.0 AND no CableCheckRes with OK+EVSEProcessing=='Finished' and no PreChargeReq has been seen since t_first_CableCheckReq AND max gap between consecutive events in that window < 10 s (no capture seam) AND the dialog opened inside this capture (a supportedAppProtocolReq or SessionSetupReq preceded the CableCheckReq)
  - why: V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout is 40 s measured from the FIRST CableCheckReq of the charging session; at 38 s the charger has already breached its own performance time. A cable check still unfinished at 40 s is a normative timeout, and on DC captures it is the dominant isolation-fault signature.
  - FP risk: Two real traps: (a) a ring-file seam or an idle hole inside the window inflates the span - hence the continuity gate; (b) this firmware does not always populate EVSEProcessing on CableCheckRes, so completion must also be inferred from the first PreChargeReq ([V2G2-525]). Anchor the fault at t0+40 s, not at the end of the phase.
- `hr_ongoing_timeout_60s_non_auth` → COMM_STALL / EVSE_PROCESSING_STALL (confidence 0.88)
  - condition: phase in {CableCheck, ChargeParameterDiscovery} AND (t - t_first_Ongoing_of_that_phase_in_this_dialog) >= 60.0 AND no response of that phase has carried EVSEProcessing=='Finished' since AND max inter-event gap in the window < 10 s
  - why: V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout is 60 s from the FIRST Ongoing response of the run; the charger itself was obliged to send a FAILED and stop at 55 s. Passing 60 s with no Finished is a confirmed processing stall on the charger side.
  - FP risk: ContractAuthentication is deliberately excluded: an EIM authorization loop is legitimately waiting for a human to present a card, and DIN has no Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction value to say so. The t0 must be the FIRST Ongoing, not the most recent - a detector that resets on every poll never fires. Reset the timer on a re-dialog.
- `hr_precharge_timeout_diverged` → PRECHARGE_FAULT (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: (t - t_first_PreChargeReq_of_dialog) >= 7.0 AND no PowerDeliveryReq seen since AND the latest PreChargeRes has |ev_target_v - evse_v| > max(20.0, 0.05*ev_target_v) AND that gap has not shrunk by >=20 % over the last 3 s AND both voltages are inside [0,1000] AND max inter-event gap in the window < 3 s
  - why: V2G_EVCC_PreCharge_Timeout is 7 s from the first PreChargeReq (the charger's own performance budget is 5 s). Combining the expired timer with a non-converging output voltage separates a charger that cannot slew to the pack voltage from a vehicle whose internal acceptance threshold is simply strict.
  - FP risk: The completion criterion is an EV-internal measurement with no protocol flag and the standard publishes no numeric voltage tolerance, so the 20 V / 5 % band is a heuristic and must be documented as such. Pre-charge always starts with a huge gap, hence the convergence test rather than the gap alone.
- `hr_overvoltage_beyond_declared_limit` → EVSE_FAULT / OVERVOLTAGE (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: 3 consecutive CurrentDemandRes (or PreChargeRes) rows, none FAILED-coded, each with evse_v > 1.02 * min(ev_max_v_held, evse_max_v_held) AND every value used inside Table 68 ranges (V<=1000) AND phys_range_violation_count==0 for the session so far
  - why: Delivering above the pack's own declared EVMaximumVoltageLimit, or above the charger's own declared maximum, is never healthy and is the electrical condition behind FAILED_ChargingVoltageOutOfRange.
  - FP risk: A stale or mis-scaled limit (wrong PhysicalValueType multiplier) manufactures phantom over-voltage - hence the range gate, the 2 % margin and the three-sample requirement. Never evaluate on a FAILED response.
- `hr_failed_close_response` → SHUTDOWN_FAULT (confidence 0.92)
  - condition: event.msg in {'SessionStopRes','WeldingDetectionRes'} AND event.resp_code.upper().startswith('FAILED')
  - why: At the end of a DC session these two responses go FAILED when the charger could not measure control pilot State B - the vehicle did not release the pilot after stopping. It is a shutdown-sequence / connector-unlock fault and a welding-risk indicator, materially different from a comms error even though it is also a FAILED code.
  - FP risk: Overlaps hr_response_code_failed by construction; it exists to give the right fault family. Correlate with the WeldingDetectionRes EVSEPresentVoltage before calling it a welded contactor.
- `hr_header_fault_notification` → PROTOCOL_FAILED / DECODE (confidence 0.85)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND json.loads(event.extra or '{}').get('FaultCode') is present and non-empty
  - why: The optional header Notification exists solely to report that the peer could not decode a V2G message (ParsingError, NoTLSRootCertificatAvailable, UnknownError). During normal operation the element is simply absent, so any presence is abnormal and usually implicates the schema/dialect rather than the hardware.
  - FP risk: Depends on the extractor actually surfacing the header element rather than a body tag of the same name; verify on a sample before alarming. FaultMsg often carries a vendor string that identifies the exact failure.
- `hr_physical_range_violation` → DECODE_ERROR (confidence 0.95)
  - condition: event.kind=='v2g' AND not resp_code FAILED-prefixed AND any decoded quantity outside Table 68: voltage outside [0,1000] V, current outside [0,400] A, or soc outside [0,100] %
  - why: After the multiplier has been applied, values outside the standard's declared ranges are far more often a decoder/scaling/dialect fault than a real electrical excursion. Firing this rule must invalidate the session's other electrical features rather than accuse the charger.
  - FP risk: Must be evaluated BEFORE any comparison-based electrical rule, or range errors masquerade as regulation faults. Do not evaluate on FAILED responses, whose fields are arbitrary filler.
- `hr_ev_ready_never_true` → EV_ERROR / INCOMPATIBILITY (confidence 0.75)
  - condition: the dialog reached a PowerDeliveryReq with ChargeProgress/ReadyToChargeState indicating Start AND ev_ready was populated (non-empty) on >=5 earlier rows AND was never 'true' in this dialog
  - why: Table 100 explicitly licenses EVReady=FALSE as the alternative to FAILED_ChargingSystemIncompatibility, so a vehicle that never asserts EVReady while the loop is being opened is declaring the station unusable. A detector that watches only EVErrorCode misses half of these aborts because the code is optional.
  - FP risk: Firmware that never populates or always mis-populates EVReady would fire constantly - the >=5 populated rows gate is essential, and the rule should be validated against the fleet's EVReady distribution before it is armed in production.

### ground-truth changes the standard implies (12)

- Stop reading evse_status / isolation / ev_err / voltages out of any message whose ResponseCode starts with FAILED. When rc is FAILED, record only the PROTOCOL_FAILED marker for that row.
  - today: label_session() scans resp_code, evse_status, isolation and ev_err on the same row independently, so one FAILED response can add phantom EVSE_FAULT / ISOLATION_FAULT / EV_ERROR markers at the same instant.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-735]/[V2G2-736]: on a FAILED ResponseCode the EVCC shall ignore all other parameters, and the SECC fills every mandatory parameter with arbitrary XSD-conformant values chosen to minimise message size. Those fields are meaningless by design.
  - risk: Very low. It only removes co-located duplicate families; the session stays faulty via PROTOCOL_FAILED. Small chance a charger does fill real values and a genuinely distinct family is lost from the taxonomy (not from the binary label).
- Add 'NoData' to BENIGN_EV_ERR, and treat 'Reserved_A/B/C' as an implementation defect rather than an EV_ERROR fault.
  - today: BENIGN_EV_ERR = {'', 'NO_ERROR'} - so a vehicle that has not yet determined its operating state (NoData, normal in the first DC requests) is labelled EV_ERROR, as is any Reserved_* vendor code.
  - under ISO: Table 100 defines NoData as 'the vehicle has not yet determined its operating state'; Reserved_A..C carry no defined semantics. Neither is a fault annunciation.
  - risk: Low, and it reduces recall slightly if any session's only marker was NoData. EV_ERROR is a small family (27 sessions in the legacy labels), so measure the delta before accepting it.
- Move the CableCheck-stuck threshold from 60 s to the normative 40 s, anchor the fault at t0+40 s, and treat the first PreChargeReq (not only EVSEProcessing=='Finished') as proof the check completed.
  - today: CABLECHECK_STUCK_S = 60.0, with the fault anchored at t0+60; the end of the phase is taken as the first PreChargeReq or PowerDeliveryReq.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_CableCheck_Timeout is 40 s from the first CableCheckReq of the charging session (Table 111, [V2G2-700..702]); the SECC has already breached its own 38 s performance time by then. 60 s is not a published number anywhere in the cable-check chain.
  - risk: Medium: it will label additional sessions faulty in the 40-60 s band and it moves every existing CableCheck fault time 20 s earlier, which changes the earliness component of the score for every detector. Requires a continuity gate so a ring-file seam inside the phase does not create a synthetic 40 s span.
- Re-anchor COMM_FREEZE on the standard's own timers instead of a flat 5 s: (a) a CurrentDemandReq with no CurrentDemandRes before the stream ends is the hard case (250 ms EVCC message timeout); (b) any response-to-next-request gap >= 60 s is a hard case (SECC sequence timeout); (c) 5-60 s stalls become a graded severity feature, not a fault family, unless corroborated by an unanswered request or a FAILED code.
  - today: FREEZE_S = 5.0 - any inter-message gap > 5 s strictly inside the CurrentDemand loop is labelled COMM_FREEZE, anchored at prev_t+5.
  - under ISO: There is no 5 s constant in the DC loop. Table 109 gives V2G_EVCC_Msg_Timeout(CurrentDemandReq)=0,25 s and V2G_SECC_Msg_Performance_Time(CurrentDemandRes)=0,025 s for the request/response pair, V2G_EVCC_Sequence_Performance_Time=40 s and V2G_SECC_Sequence_Timeout=60 s for the gap between exchanges.
  - risk: Highest-variance change of the set. Applying 0,25 s to every pair would explode the positive rate (real hardware breaches performance times constantly), so it must be applied only to requests that are never answered. Demoting the 5-60 s band loses genuine short stalls that operators do care about - recommend keeping them as a separate, clearly-named DEGRADED class rather than deleting them.
- Stop counting a SessionStopRes as a graceful close when its ResponseCode is FAILED, and add a SHUTDOWN_FAULT family for a FAILED SessionStopRes or WeldingDetectionRes.
  - today: graceful = (stop_res_t is not None) - the ResponseCode of the SessionStopRes is never inspected, so a FAILED close counts as clean and suppresses SESSION_ABORT.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-920..922]: the SECC answers WeldingDetectionRes/SessionStopRes with FAILED when it could not measure control pilot State B, i.e. the vehicle never released the pilot. Only ResponseCode='OK' ends the session without error.
  - risk: Low and strictly additive; the PROTOCOL_FAILED marker already fires on those rows, so the binary label rarely changes - the value is a correct family and a correct graceful_close flag.
- Split SESSION_ABORT by corroboration: hard abort when the missing SessionStop is accompanied by a TCP RST, a preceding FAILED response, a request left unanswered longer than its Table 109 per-message timeout, or a re-dialog; otherwise (last messages a healthy CurrentDemand loop, or ChargingComplete=true / StopCharging already seen) label it UNCLEAN_CLOSE and exclude it from the faulty set.
  - today: Any session with no SessionStopRes is SESSION_ABORT, anchored at the RST or at the last row.
  - under ISO: A TCP teardown before SessionStopRes is normatively an error - but the standard also gives two benign look-alikes: a CP State E/F event ([V2G2-737]) makes the EVCC stop the session and drop the connection with no error code at all, and a Pause-type SessionStop deliberately tears the transport down. Neither is a charger defect.
  - risk: HIGHEST-impact change: SESSION_ABORT is 624 of 789 legacy faults, so re-classifying the uncorroborated subset moves the fault base rate substantially and invalidates comparisons against earlier leaderboards. On this fleet the capture almost always ends at a ring-file boundary followed by hours of idle, which is why the uncorroborated case is so common. Do this as a separate labelled field first (keep both labels) and measure before switching the ground truth.
- Require our own EVSE to have participated before labelling SLAC_FAILURE: at least one .CNF from the charger side (CM_SLAC_PARM.CNF / SLAC_MATCH.CNF / ATTEN_CHAR.RSP) or an SDP request or a TCP SYN, not merely N broadcast SLAC frames.
  - today: SLAC_FAILURE is raised whenever there are >=3 hpav SLAC/ATTEN_CHAR rows and no V2G rows at all.
  - under ISO: SDP/SLAC bring-up is per-link; the standard assumes the frames belong to the link being observed. The powerline here is shared, so broadcast-only frames with no response from our SECC belong to the neighbouring connector's EV.
  - risk: Medium-low: reduces the SLAC_FAILURE family (41 legacy sessions) and could hide a genuine failure where the charger's SLAC responder is entirely dead - but that case also produces no .CNF, so add a separate 'no SECC response to any SLAC' family rather than silently dropping it.
- Add an EVSE_PROCESSING_STALL family: EVSEProcessing stuck at 'Ongoing' for >=60 s from the FIRST Ongoing of a CableCheck or ChargeParameterDiscovery run, and explicitly do NOT apply it to ContractAuthentication.
  - today: EVSEProcessing is not used by the labeller at all; a session stuck for minutes in a ChargeParameterDiscovery or authorization poll loop is only caught indirectly (as an abort at the end).
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_Ongoing_Timeout is 60 s from the first Ongoing response; the SECC was obliged to emit a FAILED and stop at 55 s. But an EIM authorization loop is legitimately waiting for a human, which ISO marks with Ongoing_WaitingForCustomerInteraction - a value DIN does not have.
  - risk: Adds new positives and needs a re-dialog reset or a retry inside one sessionize chunk reports a fake multi-minute stall. Excluding authorization is essential or every slow driver becomes a fault.
- Add a PRECHARGE_FAULT family (pre-charge phase past 7 s with EVSEPresentVoltage not converging on EVTargetVoltage) and explicitly record EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent as a NON-fault suppressor rather than leaving it unclassified.
  - today: Neither pre-charge duration nor UtilityInterruptEvent is considered; a charger that cannot ramp to the pack voltage is only caught later, as an abort.
  - under ISO: V2G_EVCC_PreCharge_Timeout is 7 s from the first PreChargeReq (SECC performance time 5 s). EVSE_UtilityInterruptEvent is defined as an operator/utility-requested temporary load reduction - deliberate curtailment, not equipment failure.
  - risk: The 7 s timeout is short and the completion criterion is an EV-internal measurement with no protocol flag, so a duration-only rule would over-fire; requiring non-convergence is a heuristic band (ISO publishes no numeric voltage tolerance) and must be documented as such.
- Make ChargingComplete / BulkChargingComplete / EVSENotification=StopCharging explicit negative evidence: a session whose last CurrentDemandReq carried ChargingComplete=true and which then ran PowerDelivery(Stop) -> [WeldingDetection] -> SessionStop must never be labelled faulty by the stall or shortfall logic, however abruptly the current fell.
  - today: The docstring says EVSE_Shutdown + StopCharging is deliberately not a fault, but the completion flags are never read, so a taper or a pause at the end of a full charge can still be caught by the 5 s freeze rule inside the loop.
  - under ISO: ChargingComplete=TRUE means 100 % SOC reached and BulkChargingComplete ~80 %; the standard's own clean-termination path is PowerDelivery(Stop) -> optional WeldingDetection -> SessionStop. StopCharging followed within the delay by an orderly stop is a normal operator-initiated end.
  - risk: Very low, and it should reduce false positives. The only exposure is a charger that sets ChargingComplete spuriously to mask a fault; guard by also requiring the close sequence to complete.
- Add a DECODE_ERROR pseudo-family for Table 68 range violations and use it to invalidate (not to fault) the electrical labels of that session.
  - today: No range checking; an out-of-range voltage or current would flow straight into any comparison-based rule and could manufacture an electrical fault label.
  - under ISO: [V2G2-832]/Table 68 fix the ranges (V 0..1000, A 0..400, W 0..200000, Wh 0..200000, remaining time 0..172800 s) after the PhysicalValueType multiplier is applied; anything outside them means a wrong multiplier, wrong unit or the wrong schema applied to the capture.
  - risk: Essentially none as a data-hygiene step. Note the schema's remaining_full_min / remaining_bulk_min columns are populated from RemainingTimeToFullSoC/BulkSoC, which ISO defines in SECONDS - confirm the unit before range-checking or the check itself becomes the false positive.
- Never label a current shortfall as a fault on the strength of delivered-vs-target alone; require the announced-limit flags to be clear, EVSEMaximumCurrentLimit to be above the request, EVTargetCurrent to be at or above EVSEMinimumCurrentLimit, no UtilityInterruptEvent, no StopCharging, no completion flag, and a sustained run.
  - today: The labeller does not use current at all, which is safe; but any detector arm that adds a naive shortfall rule will mislabel routine dual-connector load sharing as a fault.
  - under ISO: EVSECurrentLimitAchieved / EVSEVoltageLimitAchieved / EVSEPowerLimitAchieved are the charger's own declaration that it is saturated, and the standard makes the unannounced shortfall (all three flags FALSE) the precondition for FAILED_ChargingCurrentdifferential. Below EVSEMinimumCurrentLimit the charger's regulation accuracy is not guaranteed, so taper deviation is expected.
  - risk: On this fleet load sharing has been observed WITHOUT EVSECurrentLimitAchieved being set, so even the fully-gated version can false-positive; keep it a feature and let the model weigh it rather than promoting it to a ground-truth family. EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance is absent from the schema, so the tolerance band is unavoidably heuristic.

### not checkable from our capture

NOTIF-STOPCHARGING-MAXDELAY / ENUM-STOPCHARGING-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / NOTIF-RENEGOTIATION-MAXDELAY / ENUM-RENEGOTIATION-WITHIN-MAXDELAY / FIELD-NOTIFICATIONMAXDELAY / SEQ-AC-EVSE-TRIGGERED-RENEG / RENEG-EVSENOTIFICATION-MAXDELAY - NotificationMaxDelay is not extracted (not a column and not in EXTRA_TAGS), so the StopCharging / ReNegotiation deadline can only be approximated with a fixed heuristic window., ELEC-EVSECURRENTREGULATIONTOLERANCE - EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance is not extracted, so the only standards-traceable current-tracking band (tolerance + ripple/2) cannot be built; EVSEPeakCurrentRipple IS available in extra, the tolerance half must be a documented heuristic., ELEC-EVSEMAXIMUMPOWERLIMIT / MSG-CURRENTDEMANDRES-LIMITFLAGS (power half) / PERF-POWER-FALLING-EDGE-5S-10PCT (power form) - EVSEMaximumPowerLimit and EVMaximumPowerLimit are not extracted; only the EVSEPowerLimitAchieved boolean survives, so delivered power can only be compared against V*I derived proxies of the voltage/current maxima., CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-SASCHEDULE / SEQ-CHARGINGPROFILE-WITHIN-LIMITS / SEQ-EVSE-MUST-FULFIL-PROFILE / SEQ-RENEG-PROFILE-CHOICE / RC-FAILED-CHARGINGPROFILEINVALID / RC-FAILED-TARIFFSELECTIONINVALID / SASCHEDULETUPLEID-UNIQUE / SEQ-UNIQUE-SASCHEDULETUPLEID / ELEC-SASCHEDULETUPLEID-RANGE / SCHED-ENTRY-COUNT-LIMITS / PERF-CHARGINGPROFILE-SLOT-120S / ELEC-CHARGINGPROFILE-120S-GRACE / STRUCT-RELATIVETIMEINTERVAL-RANGES / CHARGINGPROFILE-MAX-24-ENTRIES - ChargingProfile, SAScheduleTuple/SAScheduleTupleID, PMaxScheduleEntry and ProfileEntry are not extracted at all., All control-pilot rules: CP-EVCC-STATE-E-F-ABORT, CP-DC-EV-STATE-CD-BEFORE-CABLECHECK, CP-DC-EV-STATE-B-AFTER-STOP, CP-DC-EV-NO-STATE-CHANGE-DURING-LOOP, CP-DC-EVSE-5PCT-DUTY-WHOLE-SESSION, DC-CP-STATE-HOLD, DC-EVSE-PWM-5-PERCENT-WHOLE-SESSION, AC-CP-STATE-250MS-DEADLINES, AC-EV-CPSTATE-250MS-AROUND-POWERDELIVERY, AC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-3S, AC-SECC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-CONSTRAINTS, CP-STATE-EF-STOPS-SESSION, SEQ-EVCC-CP-STATE-EF-STOP, EVCC-STOP-ON-CP-STATE-E-OR-F - no analogue control-pilot signalling in the capture; only their message-level consequences (a FAILED CableCheckRes / WeldingDetectionRes / SessionStopRes) are observable., MSG-RECEIPTREQUIRED / SEQ-DC-CURRENTDEMAND-RECEIPTREQUIRED / FSM-CURRENTDEMANDRES-RECEIPT-NEXT / SEQ-RECEIPTREQUIRED-LOOP / RECEIPTREQUIRED-PER-IDENTIFICATION-MODE - ReceiptRequired is neither a column nor an EXTRA_TAG., ELEC-METERREADING-RANGE / MSG-METERINGRECEIPTREQ-ECHO / RC-FAILED-METERINGSIGNATURENOTVALID - MeterInfo / MeterReading are not extracted., ELEC-PHYSICALVALUETYPE-ENCODING (multiplier range -3..+3) - the extractor collapses Multiplier and Value into a float, so an out-of-range multiplier is only visible indirectly as a Table 68 range violation., SEQ-SESSIONSTOP-CHARGINGSESSION-TERMINATE-PAUSE / CONN-DLINK-PAUSE-ON-CHARGINGSESSION-PAUSE / ENUM-CHARGINGSESSION-PAUSE / TERM-PAUSE-VS-TERMINATE-DATALINK / SEQ-RESUME-EVCC-PARAMS / SEQ-RESUME-SECC-PARAMS - DIN 70121 SessionStopReq carries no ChargingSession element (the extractor lists it in EXTRA_TAGS but it will be absent on this fleet), so Pause-vs-Terminate cannot be distinguished and its absence must not be flagged., All TLS / certificate / PnC rules: TLS-VERSION-AND-ROLE, TLS-CIPHER-SUITES, TLS-ABORT-ON-CERT-VERIFICATION-FAILURE, SEC-TLS-DOWNGRADE-PROHIBITED, CERT-SIZE-DEPTH-VALIDITY, SEC-OCSP-AND-PAIRING-CONSTANTS, RC-FAILED-CERT* , RC-FAILED-CHALLENGEINVALID, MSG-AUTHORIZATIONREQ-GENCHALLENGE, MSG-CERTIFICATEUPDATERES-RETRYCOUNTER - DIN 70121 EIM has no TLS and no certificates, and none of these fields are extracted., SDP wire-format rules: SDP-REQ-PAYLOAD-TYPE-AND-LENGTH, SDP-RES-PAYLOAD-FORMAT, SDP-SECURITY-TRANSPORT-ENCODING, SDP-RESPONSE-UNICAST-BACK-TO-CLIENT, SDP-REQUEST-FORMAT-AND-PORTS, V2GTP-USE-PORT-FROM-LAST-SDP-RESPONSE, EVCC-USES-SDP-RETURNED-TCP-PORT - the schema keeps only an SDP info line, not the payload bytes, ports or addresses., V2GTP framing rules: V2GTP-HEADER-VERSION-PATTERN, V2GTP-ONE-MESSAGE-PER-PDU, V2GTP-HEADER-CHECK-ORDER-AND-DISCARD, V2GTP-PAYLOAD-TYPES, V2GTP-TCP-PORTS-DYNAMIC-RANGE, TCP-NO-URGENT-POINTER, IPV6-NO-FRAGMENTATION, ICMPV6-MANDATORY-SET - no raw bytes, IP layer or TCP flags beyond RST / RETX / LOST., EXI configuration rules: EXI-HEADER-NO-COOKIE-DISCARD-NONCONFORMING, EXI-OPTION-VALUEPARTITIONCAPACITY-0, EXI-PROFILE-BUILTIN-GRAMMARS-ZERO, EXI-SCHEMA-DEVIATION-HANDLING - only the derived Wireshark `validation` result is available (healthy value 'Successful'), not the EXI header or options., MSG-SESSIONSETUPRES-CONTENT (EVSEID checks) - EVSEID is in EXTRA_TAGS but is EMPTY on this firmware, so per-outlet attribution and the ZZ00000 / length checks cannot run., SEQ-EVCC-SEQUENCE-PERFORMANCE-TIME / TIMING-SETUP-EVCC-COMMSETUP-TIMEOUT-20S anchored at D-LINK_READY - there is no data-link-ready indication in the schema; the SLAC_MATCH.CNF hpav line or the first V2G row is only a proxy, so a marginal 19-21 s setup measurement must not be reported as a violation., ELEC-REMAININGTIME-RANGE - implementable only after resolving a unit ambiguity: the columns are named remaining_full_min / remaining_bulk_min but are populated from RemainingTimeToFullSoC / RemainingTimeToBulkSoC, which the standard defines in seconds (0..172800).


# Completeness critic — 50 rules the assigned readers missed

I audited the sections nobody was assigned (clause 8.6, Annex A, Annex C, Annex D) plus a re-sweep of 8.7/8.8, and cross-checked every [V2G2-] ID appearing in 8.7-8.8 against the 773 extracted rules.

WHERE THE REAL GAPS ARE

1. Annex C (normative schema) was the largest untouched source of machine-checkable constraints. The extraction has value ranges (Table 68) but almost no *cardinality* or *string-length* rules. I added 20+: sessionID <=8 octets; evseID 7..37 chars; SAScheduleList <=3 tuples; PMaxSchedule/SalesTariff <=1024 entries; SelectedServiceList <=16; ServiceParameterList 255x16; SupportedEnergyTransferMode <=6; PaymentOptionList 1..2; ChargingProfile <=24 entries with phases 1..3; RelativeTimeInterval start<=16777214 / duration<=86400; certificate 800 B, sub-certs 4, root IDs 20, genChallenge exactly 16 B, eMAID 14..15, meterID <=32, faultMsg <=64; unitSymbolType is exactly {h,m,s,A,V,W,Wh}; and the exact mandatory/optional element sets for CurrentDemandReq/Res, CableCheck/PreCharge/WeldingDetection, DC_EVSEChargeParameter, DC_EVChargeParameter and MeterInfo. Two of these correct existing rules: MeteringReceiptReq's SAScheduleTupleID is minOccurs=0 (the existing MSG-METERINGRECEIPTREQ-ECHO overstates it), and ChargeParameterDiscoveryRes must carry EVSEChargeParameter even when EVSEProcessing=Ongoing.

2. Clause 8.6 (Message Sets) was entirely absent from the extraction and is the missing *scoping* layer for everything else. [V2G2-659..668] make Table 104's M/O/- markings normative — in particular a party shall NOT support anything marked '-'. [V2G2-402..405] make the active Message Set a function of two observable fields (SelectedPaymentOption + RequestedEnergyTransferMode). The single most useful consequence for this project: in DC Charging EIM — which is exactly what a DIN 70121 capture is — Table 104 marks MeterInfo, MeterID, MeterReading, SigMeterReading, TMeter, ReceiptRequired and the header Signature as '-'. So the whole MeteringReceipt branch and all XML signing are out-of-set for these captures, and [V2G2-762]/[V2G2-763] say only the FIRST SAScheduleTuple is meaningful in EIM. Also new: [V2G2-422]/[V2G2-432]/[V2G2-433] (a service must be detailed before it can be selected), Table 105-107 ServiceID/ParameterSetID allocation, [V2G2-774] VAS-for-the-whole-session.

3. Genuinely skipped in 8.7/8.8: [V2G2-677] (SECC shall set EVSENotification='None' when it is not requesting anything — the extraction has only the EVCC-side -678, so the "None is nominal" baseline was never anchored); [V2G2-688]'s prohibition on SASchedule while Ongoing; the ten symmetric SECC FAILED branches [V2G2-546/549/552/555/558/561/564/566/569/572]; [V2G2-919]/[V2G2-920]'s "SECC tries to measure CP State B and waits at most one performance time"; and the observation that Table 110/111 define V2G_SECC_CableCheck_Timer (38 s) and V2G_SECC_PreCharge_Timer (5 s) but NO requirement acts on their expiry — only the EVCC's 40 s / 7 s are enforceable, so 38 s / 5 s should be graded as warnings.

4. Annex A and Annex D yield nothing normative. Annex A is one 37-page rotated informative table mapping elements to Part 1 use case IDs (duplicate of Table 104's membership); Annex D is examples. I recorded both as "do not spend more effort here", with the note that Annex D's three EXI byte streams are useful decoder regression vectors (ISO namespace only).

5. Document defects found: Table 104 lists a DC_EVStatus element "EVRESSConditioning" that does not exist in the Annex C schema (a dissector written from Table 104 would mis-align every DC_EVStatus); [V2G2-881] cites Table 104 where it means Table 100; [V2G2-921]/[V2G2-922] cite Table 103 where they mean Table 109; [V2G2-541] says "ServiceSetupReq" for SessionSetupReq; [V2G2-791]/[V2G2-798] and clause 8.8.2 write "FAIL" for "FAILED"; [V2G2-840]/[V2G2-841] write "Renegotiation" for the schema literal "Renegotiate"; [V2G2-854]/[V2G2-855] write "ServicePaymentSelectReq".

DIN 70121 APPLICABILITY — RULES IN THE EXISTING 773 THAT WILL NOT FIRE ON THESE CAPTURES

AC-only (drop or gate off RequestedEnergyTransferMode): every CP-AC-*, AC-*, SEQ-AC-*, TIMING-AC-*, FSM-CHARGINGSTATUSRES-*, FSM-CPDRES-AC-FINISHED-NEXT, FSM-POWERDELIVERYRES-AC-START-NEXT, FSM-METERINGRECEIPTRES-AC-NEXT, TIMING-EVCC-STOP-METERINGRECEIPT-AC, TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-CHARGINGSTATUSREQ, TIMING-MSGPERF-SECC-CHARGINGSTATUSRES, ELEC-RCD-INFORMATIONAL, SEQ-AC-EIM-NORMAL-FULL, SEQ-AC-PNC-NORMAL-FULL, AC-CP-TIMING-250MS, AC-CP-STATE-250MS-DEADLINES, AC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-3S, AC-PWM-AND-AUTHORIZATION, AC-SECC-CONTACTOR-*, and [V2G2-691] (ReceiptRequired=FALSE in EIM applies to ChargingStatusRes, an AC-only message).

Contactor-error family is AC-only AND absent from DIN's enum: RC-CONTACTOR-FAILED-ON-START/-STOP, RC-CONTACTOR-ERROR-PWM-100, RC-FAILED-CONTACTORERROR, ENUM-RC-FAILED-CONTACTORERROR, ABNORM-CONTACTOR-ERROR, RESPONSECODE-CONTACTOR-ERROR, ELEC-CONTACTOR-CLOSE-TIMING, SECC-NO-CONTACTOR-BEFORE-START, RC-TABLE112-CONTACTORERROR-AND-CERTNOTALLOWED. DIN 70121 has no FAILED_ContactorError literal.

PnC / TLS / certificate-only (never present in a DIN DC EIM capture): all RC-FAILED-CERT*, RC-OK-CERTIFICATEEXPIRESSOON, ENUM-RC-FAILED-CERTIFICATE*, ENUM-RC-FAILED-CONTRACTCANCELED, ENUM-RC-FAILED-CHALLENGEINVALID, ENUM-RC-FAILED-NOCERTIFICATEAVAILABLE, ENUM-RC-FAILED-CERTCHAINERROR, ENUM-RC-FAILED-METERINGSIGNATURENOTVALID, CERT-*, SEC-*, TLS-*, SEQ-PNC-CERTIFICATE-BRANCHES, FSM-CERTIFICATE*-NEXT, FSM-PAYMENTDETAILSRES-NEXT, TIMING-*-CERTINSTALL/-CERTUPDATE/-PAYMENTDETAILS, MSG-AUTHORIZATIONREQ-GENCHALLENGE, MSG-CERTIFICATEUPDATERES-RETRYCOUNTER, SEQ-DC-PNC-NORMAL-FULL, and every MeteringReceipt rule (SEQ-DC-METERINGRECEIPT-*, FSM-METERINGRECEIPTRES-DC-NEXT, SEQ-DC-CURRENTDEMAND-RECEIPTREQUIRED, MSG-RECEIPTREQUIRED, MSG-METERINGRECEIPTREQ-ECHO, METERREADING-RANGE-WH, RECEIPTREQUIRED-PER-IDENTIFICATION-MODE, SEQ-RECEIPTREQUIRED-LOOP).

Structurally absent from the DIN 70121 dialect — these will produce false "missing field" alerts unless gated:
- PowerDeliveryReq carries a boolean ReadyToChargeState, NOT a three-valued ChargeProgress. Everything keyed on ChargeProgress=Renegotiate is inapplicable (SEQ-CHARGEPROGRESS-RENEGOTIATION-RULES, RENEG-*, SEQ-DC-RENEG-*, FSM-POWERDELIVERYRES-RENEGOTIATE-NEXT, RC-FAILED-RENEGOTIATE-WITHOUT-START, ENUM-CHARGEPROGRESS-RENEGOTIATE, SEQ-DC-EVCC-RENEGOTIATION-PATHS, ENUM-EVSENOTIFICATION-RENEGOTIATION / NOTIF-RENEGOTIATION-MAXDELAY). Start/Stop map onto ReadyToChargeState true/false.
- DIN SessionStopReq has no ChargingSession element: SEQ-SESSIONSTOP-CHARGINGSESSION-TERMINATE-PAUSE, MSG-SESSIONSTOPREQ-CONTENT, ENUM-CHARGINGSESSION-*, SESSION-PAUSE-VS-TERMINATE, TERM-PAUSE-VS-TERMINATE-DATALINK, TERM-RESUME-OLDSESSIONJOINED, CONN-DLINK-PAUSE-ON-CHARGINGSESSION-PAUSE, STATE-*-SESSIONSTOP-PAUSE-DLINK are all inapplicable.
- DIN EVSEProcessingType has only {Finished, Ongoing}: ENUM-EVSEPROC-ONGOING-WAITINGFORCUSTOMER, SEQ-AUTH-ONGOING-WAITINGFORCUSTOMER, AUTH-EVSEPROCESSING-EIM-CUSTOMER-INTERACTION, AUTH-EVSEPROCESSING-VALUES-EIM-VS-PNC do not apply as written; the observable equivalent is ContractAuthenticationRes stuck at Ongoing until the 60 s Ongoing timeout.
- DIN CurrentDemandRes has no EVSEID, SAScheduleTupleID, MeterInfo or ReceiptRequired; DIN SessionSetupRes types EVSEID as hexBinary, not a 7..37-char string. So MSG-SESSIONSETUPRES-EVSEID-DC-INCONSISTENCY and my SCHEMA-EVSEID-LENGTH-7-TO-37 are ISO-only length checks.
- DIN's ResponseCode enum differs: it has FAILED_EVSEPresentVoltageToLow (no ISO equivalent, and a genuinely useful pre-charge fault code that nothing in the 773 covers) and FAILED_WrongEnergyTransferType; it lacks OK_CertificateExpiresSoon, FAILED_NoChargeServiceSelected, FAILED_ContactorError, FAILED_CertificateNotAllowedAtThisEVSE and FAILED_CertificateRevoked. ENUM-RC-COMPLETE-SET ("exactly 26 literals") is ISO-only.
- No ServiceDetailReq/Res in DIN: TIMING-MSGTO-EVCC-SERVICEDETAILREQ, TIMING-MSGPERF-SECC-SERVICEDETAILRES, TIMING-EVCC-STOP-SERVICEDETAIL, FSM-SERVICEDETAILRES-NEXT, RC-FAILED-SERVICEIDINVALID are inapplicable.
- SalesTariff / ChargingProfile / falling-edge power rules (PERF-CHARGINGPROFILE-SLOT-120S, ELEC-CHARGINGPROFILE-120S-GRACE, PERF-POWER-FALLING-EDGE-5S-10PCT, ELEC-POWER-ADJUST-5S-10PCT, ELEC-POWERDELIVERY-FALLINGEDGE, SCHED-*, SASCHEDULE-PROFILE-CONSTRAINTS) are AC/tariff-oriented and largely vestigial in DIN DC captures, where SAScheduleList is normally a single trivial tuple.

Finally, one framing correction worth carrying forward: clause 8.6.1 NOTE 3 says an "M" in Table 104 means the party must SUPPORT the parameter, not that it will transmit it. Only [V2G2-662] and [V2G2-667] create send obligations. Any rule that alerts on the mere absence of a schema-optional element (EVMaximumPowerLimit, RemainingTimeToFullSoC, EVSEMaximumVoltageLimit in CurrentDemandRes, EVSECurrentRegulationTolerance, EVSEIsolationStatus, EVSETimeStamp in SessionSetupRes) will generate false positives.

| SCHEMA-HEADER-COMPOSITION-STRICT | other |  | MessageHeaderType is a sequence of SessionID (mandatory), Notification (minOccurs=0) and xmlsig:Signature (minOccurs=0). Order is fixed. A message header without SessionID is not decodable; a header carrying Notification signals a parse/decode fault reported by the sender. |
| SCHEMA-SESSIONID-MAX-8-BYTES | enum |  | SessionID is typed sessionIDType = xs:hexBinary with maxLength 8, i.e. a SessionID is 1..8 octets. The SessionID '0' of the first SessionSetupReq and the SECC-generated SessionID must both fit this bound. |
| SCHEMA-EVSEID-LENGTH-7-TO-37 | enum |  | EVSEID is typed evseIDType = xs:string with minLength 7 and maxLength 37. It appears in SessionSetupRes and (ISO only) in ChargingStatusRes and CurrentDemandRes. An EVSEID shorter than 7 characters is schema-invalid. |

| SCHEMA-SCHEDULE-ENTRY-MAX-1024 | enum |  | PMaxScheduleType allows up to 1024 PMaxScheduleEntry elements and SalesTariffType up to 1024 SalesTariffEntry elements; SalesTariffEntry may carry 0..3 ConsumptionCost, each with up to 3 Cost elements.
